# iOS vs Windows parity map

- Date: 2026-11-15 (Phase 1 map); re-baselined against the Windows working tree and the 44 user decisions recorded 2026-11-16; final outcome of the fix phase recorded 2026-11-18 (every item below ends with its final `outcome`); the decisions of 2026-11-19 on the open blockers are recorded and applied (23 items: 21 keep-iOS decisions, 4 code changes; section E); the first successful GitHub Actions runs (2026-11-20 UTC) and what they cannot check are recorded in [What was and was not verified](#what-was-and-was-not-verified)
- Final outcome (2026-11-19): 220 items = 172 fixed + 25 fixed-ui-unverified (ported; UI part needs a device check) + 0 blocked-needs-decision (the 8 items kept for your decision were decided on 2026-11-19, iOS behaviour kept, and are now wont-fix) + 21 wont-fix + 1 cannot-reproduce + 1 no-longer-divergent (2026-11-18 it was 174 / 23 / 8 / 13 / 1 / 1). See [Summary](#summary), [Decisions of 2026-11-19](#decisions-of-2026-11-19) and [Still open](#still-open).
- Windows repo (source of truth, read-only): `OSTVisualizerLicense`. The map was made at HEAD `dceb47d`; the re-baselined working tree (see below) was committed there later as `6d4786f` ("fix: enforce consistent boolean exports and preserve meshes on failed unions") and the Windows tree was clean at `6d4786f` when this file was finalised (2026-11-18, read-only git)
- iOS repo: `OSTVisualizer`, branch `ios-parity-sync`, HEAD `42b46c3`. The fixes recorded in this file are working-tree changes on top of that HEAD (not committed when this file was written; the user commits; they were committed later, in the 7-commit push ending at `292c406`; GitHub Actions has run on that code since, see What was and was not verified)
- Machine-readable twin: `PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json` in the repo root (top level `{header, items}`; the header carries the standing rule, the status vocabulary, the final counts, the re-baseline data, the index of the decisions of 2026-11-19 (`decisions2026_11_19`) and the still-open index (`needsMyDecision`)). Wherever this document says `PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json` it means that file. Every item below carries the same id, rank and fields, and the JSON adds the full `testPlan` (expected values), `aliasTestPlans`, `files`, `question`/`options`, the `decision`, `queueNote`, `closedReason`, `rebaseline` fields and, since 2026-11-18, the final `status`, `statusBefore`, `statusCluster`, `statusWhere`, `statusNote` and `openQuestions`; since 2026-11-19 also `statusBeforeDecision`, `decisionUpdate`, `decisionTests`, `decisionTestGap`, `codeChangeDetail`, `possibleWindowsDefect` and `furtherPossibleWindowsDefects`. Test plans are not repeated here.

## Re-baseline against the Windows tree (2026-11-16)

On 2026-11-16 the Windows working tree (source of truth, read-only) had UNCOMMITTED changes made after this map was written (HEAD was still `dceb47d`). That working tree was the truth from then on; the baseline snapshot is `git diff HEAD` sha256 `f5c0376d32bc37ee1f6e4914016372c123c558c9e539261cee3ad066c6c8e56d` (18 changed paths: 2 docs, 6 production .py, 7 test files, 1 new untracked test helper, 2 QA json). No `cpp_extensions` file changed (the native boolean rule is untouched). All Windows tests pass: the six changed non-threejs test files 26 passed (10 subtests), the Three.js renderer test file 36 passed, the regression ring 280 passed (244 subtests), 0 failed; 17 new tests and 3 updated writer tests.

**Update 2026-11-18.** Windows committed this work as `6d4786f` (parent `dceb47d`), and its tree is clean at that commit. The commit changes the same 18 paths (2 docs, 6 production .py, 7 test files, 1 new test helper, 2 QA json). The Windows line citations of the six production files and of `AGENTS.md` given in the table below were re-read at `6d4786f` and still hold (`boolean_operations.py` 26-34 and 42; `base_exporter.py` 78-79, 163 and 214-215; `dxf_exporter.py` 30; `fbx_exporter.py` 29; `obj_exporter.py` 76; `mesh_factory.py` 1, 14, 91, 94-111 and 161; `AGENTS.md` 502-516). Byte-identity of the commit with the earlier snapshot was not established (only the same 18 paths and these citations were checked), and the snapshot hash below was not reproduced (the uncommitted tree no longer exists); it stays as the record of what was mapped. The iOS side of this rule is done: one shared Core boolean pass (`Core/Meshing/BooleanPass.*`, `Core/Meshing/ExportMeshSet.*`) is called by `Services/ProjectService.mm` and `Services/ExportService.mm` (items MSH-16, RB-01, RB-02, RB-03, RB-04, EXP-05, EXP-22, MSH-15, MSH-19b: all `fixed`).

What changed on Windows: one export rule for the 3D view, HTML, OBJ, DXF and FBX (a negative cuts only positives of the same `condition_type`, a fully consumed positive is dropped, `condition_type` is mandatory in the boolean pass), the writers read only the post-boolean mesh and never rebuild, an export with no surviving mesh fails before writing anything, and a wall whose drop-stub union fails keeps the plain wall and logs one warning with the takeoff UID.

Outcome for the decision items: the Windows author resolved MSH-16 (option b: one rule everywhere), EXP-22 (option b: one unified rule, reported as an Export Error) and MSH-19b (option b: keep the plain wall) in the Windows tree; the user then chose to follow the fixed behavior (section E). MSH-19 is `no-longer-divergent` (iOS applies `remove_degenerate_faces` to the union since commit 42ad10d). EXP-M02 and EXP-19 remained open questions after the re-baseline and are decided in section E (keep outward normals; adopt the colour-management and lighting constants only). Stale rows of the feature table (section G) and the FBX line of section H were corrected in this pass.

## Method

1. Twelve area mappers read the iOS code and the Windows code and tests, and executed both sides wherever the behaviour was numeric: the real iOS C++ sources were compiled on the host with MSVC (plus the vendored sqlite3.c and pugixml) and compared with the Windows Python modules and native `.pyd` extensions run with `python -B` and `-p no:cacheprovider`. Obj-C/UIKit code could not be compiled in that environment (no Xcode); those items are established by reading both sides and re-implementing the arithmetic. The first successful GitHub Actions runs (2026-11-20 UTC) later compiled every Obj-C and Obj-C++ file against the real iPhoneOS 18.5 SDK (see What was and was not verified).
2. Twelve independent verifiers re-checked every mapper divergence (re-running the numbers), corrected citations and test plans, and added missed items.
3. This synthesis merged mapper and verifier per area: CONFIRMED items are kept with the verifier corrections; AMBIGUOUS items become `needs-decision`; CANNOT_REPRODUCE items become `cannot-reproduce`; REFUTED items are dropped and counted; verifier "missed" items are added as CONFIRMED. All 12 verifiers returned results, so no item is flagged `unverified`.
4. Cross-area duplicates (the same underlying difference reported by several areas) were merged: one id is kept (its original mapper id), the other ids are listed as aliases and their test plans are kept under `aliasTestPlans` in the JSON. A merged item takes the highest impact of its members. Each kept item has ONE owner area for the fix phase (the area whose primary iOS file it lives in); when the primary file is shared, the owner is the area that owns the logic being changed.
5. Ranking: impact class first (wrong-numbers, data-safety, behavior, ui-cosmetic), then by how many users and flows are affected and how central the code is (explicit order for the big classes, then number of reporting areas), then fix before needs-decision.

Impact classes: **wrong-numbers** = a number, measurement, coordinate, exported value or stored value differs from Windows; **data-safety** = data can be lost, corrupted, overwritten or left inconsistent; **behavior** = observable logic/ordering/validation/flow differs; **ui-cosmetic** = presentation only.

Disposition: `fix` = Windows behaviour is clear and reproducible, or the user decided to port it, and the item was ported (see the final status); `wont-fix` = closed by a user decision (the item block and section E give the reason); `needs-decision` = iOS behaviour was kept and the question is listed for the user (the 8 items that were `blocked-needs-decision` until 2026-11-19; the user decided them that day, they are `wont-fix` now and the Phase 1 disposition `fix` is shown next to it); `cannot-reproduce` = cannot be reproduced on iOS (section F); `no-longer-divergent` = the difference disappeared (iOS already fixed or Windows changed; MSH-19). Testability: `core-cpp` = host unit test against Core with the Config/Tests/ harness; `needs-extraction-from-objc` = logic had to be moved from .mm into Core first; `objc-only` = UIKit/manual or device test. Verdict: result of the independent verifier (`REBASELINE` for RB-* items). Status (final, 2026-11-19; first set 2026-11-18): `fixed` = ported and covered by host unit tests and/or the Obj-C syntax check; `fixed-ui-unverified` = ported, but part of the behaviour is Obj-C/UIKit/Metal/WebKit that was syntax-checked against hand-written stubs (since the first successful CI run, 2026-11-20 UTC, it also compiles against the real iPhoneOS 18.5 SDK without errors, but nothing of it has run on a device or simulator) and needs a device or simulator check (the outcome line names it); `blocked-needs-decision` = not ported under the standing rule, iOS behaviour kept and the question listed for the user (no item has this status since 2026-11-19: the user decided the 8 items, see section E); `wont-fix`, `cannot-reproduce` and `no-longer-divergent` as above. The status each item had before the final pass (`open`, `decided`, `closed`) is `statusBefore` in the JSON; the status before the decisions of 2026-11-19 is `statusBeforeDecision`.

How to read an item block: the `Windows:` line and the `iOS (as mapped):` line describe the two sides when the map was made (the iOS side is the code BEFORE the fix, so it often describes what no longer exists); the last line, `outcome (...)`, says what was done, in which files, and what could not be verified. Phase 1 `note`, `rebaselined`, `decision`, `fix scope` and `closed` lines are kept as history. Verification wording in the outcome lines (for example "only syntax-checked against stubs") is as of 2026-11-18/19 and is not rewritten item by item: since the first successful CI run (2026-11-20 UTC) every Obj-C and Obj-C++ file also compiles against the real iPhoneOS 18.5 SDK, which still says nothing about behaviour on a device or simulator.

Feature status in section G is as reported by the mapper: `matches`, `diverges`, `unverified`, `platform-adapted`.

## Summary

Unique divergences: **220** = 216 from the Phase 1 map (from 369 reported items; 153 merged as aliases; 1 refuted; 1 synthetic split MSH-19/MSH-19b) + 4 added by the 2026-11-16 re-baseline (RB-01..RB-04). All 44 former `needs-decision` items were answered by the user on 2026-11-16 (44 of 44 decisions recorded; section E). On 2026-11-19 the user decided the 8 formal items that were still `blocked-needs-decision` and the 15 candidate items that sat inside otherwise fixed items (all but the condition-number half of DLG-FINITE-01): 21 keep-iOS decisions without a code change and 4 code changes (section E, [Decisions of 2026-11-19](#decisions-of-2026-11-19)).

| blocked-needs-decision | 0 | none left: the 8 items that were kept for your decision (DBP-04, BRW-09, EXP-12, EXP-13, EXP-16, IMP-20, LP-24, RPV-29) were decided on 2026-11-19 (iOS behaviour kept) and are now wont-fix |

What the user has not decided stays open: 3 questions (the condition-number half of DLG-FINITE-01 and the two COND-PLACE-01 notes), 10 FYI notes and 7 iOS-internal cross-cluster issues, 20 in all, listed in **[Still open](#still-open)** (section E) with the Windows file:line where one exists. The decisions of 2026-11-19 and the 18 possible Windows defects that the research found for kept items are in **[Decisions of 2026-11-19](#decisions-of-2026-11-19)** (section E).

### What was and was not verified

**Host unit tests (`Config/Tests`).** The tests live in `Config/Tests`, not in a top-level `Tests/`, because Nyxian compiles every `.cpp` outside the top-level `Config/` and `Resources/` folders. They are one CMake project; GitHub Actions runs `cmake -S Config/Tests -B build/tests -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug`, `cmake --build build/tests --parallel "$(sysctl -n hw.ncpu)"` (bounded to the runner's core count since commit 787a57d; a bare `--parallel` is an unlimited `make -j` that made the host-test step take 55 min 20 s instead of 168 s) and `ctest --test-dir build/tests --output-on-failure --no-tests=error`. Before the fix phase the repo had **0 iOS behaviour tests** (only the 10 self-tests of the harness, area Harness); now there are 1588 behaviour tests plus those 10 (22 of them were added on 2026-11-19 for the four code changes: Conditions +16, Settings +6). Since the first CI fix (commit 5dc7b2b, 2026-11-20 UTC) it is 1589 behaviour tests plus those 10, i.e. 1599 tests (1607 with the boolean tests): that fix added one Geometry test, MarkerAnchor.AnExactTieKeepsTheFirstCandidateOfTheWindowsOrder. The counts below are the snapshot of 2026-11-19. Fresh run on 2026-11-19 of the same project on the Windows host with MSVC (Debug): **1598 tests, 1598 passed, 0 failed**; with the Manifold boolean tests switched on (`-DOST_TEST_BOOLEAN=ON`): **1606 tests, 1606 passed, 0 failed**. The same sources were also built and run with clang using fused multiply-add (a host approximation of arm64/AppleClang, not the real toolchain): 1598 tests, 1598 passed, 0 failed; with the boolean tests on: 1606 tests, 1606 passed, 0 failed. The local runners are scratch scripts outside the repo. The same project also ran on GitHub Actions on the real toolchain (runner macos-15-arm64, AppleClang 17 arm64, Debug): all 12 areas pass in the two successful runs (commits 5dc7b2b and 787a57d). The CI run uses the Manifold stub (no -DOST_TEST_BOOLEAN), so its 9 boolean-dependent tests are not part of it and real Manifold is not exercised on AppleClang by the host tests. Two earlier CI runs failed in these tests (a fused multiply-add difference in a curved-mesh oracle, fixed by Core/Geometry/strict_fp.hpp, and a different marker-anchor result on arm64, probably a tie decided the other way: cause inferred, see RPV-27); the rest of the CI run is described below.

Snapshot of 2026-11-19 (MSVC). Since the first CI fix (commit 5dc7b2b) Geometry has 172 tests, so the totals are 1599 and 1607 with the boolean tests; no other area changed.

**Obj-C syntax check.** The platform-layer `.mm` and `.m` files (27 files) were checked with `zig clang -x objective-c++ -fsyntax-only` against HAND-WRITTEN stub Foundation, UIKit, QuartzCore and CoreGraphics headers: **27 of 27 files ok, 0 errors, 4 warnings** (re-run on 2026-11-19 after the four code changes: same result; the 4 warnings are baseline protocol/category warnings about the `UITableViewDataSource` methods of the condition editor, not new). This catches typos, wrong Core call signatures and missing includes. It does NOT validate against the real Apple SDK, ARC or runtime behaviour, UIKit layout and gestures, Metal shaders or any UI behaviour. The checker and the stubs are scratch tooling kept outside the repo. The compile against the real Apple SDK that followed on CI is described in the next paragraph.

**Verified on GitHub Actions (first successful runs 2026-11-20 UTC).** This authoring environment had no Xcode, no macOS, no simulator and no device, so nothing was built into an app, signed or run on a device here. The workflow `Build IPA` (`.github/workflows/build.yml`, job `build-ipa`, runner image macos-15-arm64, Xcode 16.4 (16F6), iPhoneOS 18.5 SDK, Apple clang 17.0.0) has since run every step on this code: runs 44654559214 (commit 5dc7b2b) and 44661853681 (commit 787a57d) succeeded. Two earlier runs failed in the host unit tests and never reached the later steps: 43782525569 (2026-11-16, commit 42b46c3, a fused multiply-add difference in a curved-mesh oracle, fixed by `Core/Geometry/strict_fp.hpp`) and 44642251350 (commit 292c406, `MarkerAnchor.CountTriangleAndCurvedBand` of RPV-27; the cause is inferred, see below). What the two successful runs showed: the host unit tests pass on AppleClang 17 arm64 (12 of 12 areas, Manifold stub); `libmanifold.a` and the bgfx libraries are arm64 for iOS with minos 16.0; XcodeGen generated the project (deployment target 16.0 and `-Os` read back); `xcodebuild` Release for iphoneos, unsigned, ended with BUILD SUCCEEDED (88 translation units, every one compiled with `-Os`), which is the first compile of every Obj-C and Obj-C++ file against the real iPhoneOS 18.5 SDK, with no errors; `ldid` fake signing from `Config/Entitlements.fakesign.plist` (only `get-task-allowed`); IPA packaging (artifact `OST-Visualizer-ipa`, 1,963,627 bytes). The artifact was inspected locally with a parser and nothing was executed: one app `Payload/OST Visualizer.app`, bundle id `com.fabian.ostvisualizer`, MinimumOSVersion 16.0, arm64-only executable, an ad hoc code signature whose page hashes were recomputed and equal the file; the executables of the two runs are byte-identical. Warnings in first-party code, none an error: 6 `-Wundeclared-selector` (methods defined in category or class files and only not declared where they are referenced), 1 `-Wprotocol` and 2 `-Wobjc-protocol-method-implementation` in the condition editor (the class extension adopts `UITableViewDataSource`, the category implements the methods), and 2 Info.plist warnings from `Config/Info.plist` (opening files in place is not declared; all interface orientations must be supported unless the app requires full screen), which would need new keys and so a behaviour decision of the owner.

**Still not verified (CI cannot check it).** Install and launch of the fake-signed IPA on a device (and with iRAM+ applying the memory entitlements afterwards, which the owner does); whether the owner's installer accepts the fake signature (only the structure and page hashes were checked); anything at runtime: crashes, Metal/bgfx rendering and shaders on a GPU, picking, frame time and memory, large projects; the 25 fixed-ui-unverified device checks (listed in PORTING_BLOCKERS.md), .ost/.osp open-in and the document picker; real Manifold on AppleClang through the host tests (the CI host tests use the stub) and Manifold on a device; the Debug configuration (only its settings were read back, only Release was compiled, no iOS Simulator slice was built); representative real .ost/.osp project files (the repo holds only synthetic oracles and two CAB files). arm64 floating point: the earlier host build with fused multiply-add was only an approximation and did not predict the second CI failure, the marker-anchor failure of RPV-27 (its cause, a last-bit libm difference that breaks a tie, is inferred and was not observed on arm64); the shaders were not changed or run (no shaderc, no Metal device).

## (A) Wrong numbers

66 items, global rank order. All former `needs-decision` items are decided (section E); `cannot-reproduce` items are in section F; `wont-fix` and `no-longer-divergent` items stay listed with their reason. Each block ends with its final `outcome` (status, what was done, files); the `Windows:` and `iOS (as mapped):` lines describe the two sides when the map was made, so the iOS side is the code before the fix.

#### 1. MSH-01: iOS divides all mesh coordinates and dimensions by the page scale ratio; Windows meshes in raw OST units
- owner: meshing | impact: wrong-numbers | disposition: fix | testable: needs-extraction-from-objc | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- aliases: GEO-01, LP-01, EXP-04
- Windows: `infrastructure/visualization_provider.py:114,131-139; meshing/mesh_builder.py:106; exporters/base_exporter.py:26; renderers/threejs/mesh_processor.py:45; ...`: transform_to_3d(x,y) = (-(x/1.0), y/1.0) and ost_to_real_units(v) = v/1.0 for every page. The page sf2/sf1 never scales vertices, heights, thickness or offsets; it is only used for the page size in the flip/rotate transform. 3D coordinates are therefore OST (real-world) units for all pages.
- iOS (as mapped): `Services/ProjectService.mm:119,170; Services/ExportService.mm:30,57 (sr = 1.0 / project.page_scale_ratio(page_uid), Core/Data/ProjectData.hpp:30-38) feeding Core/Meshing/ ...`: Every coordinate, height, thickness, width, depth and z offset is multiplied by 1/(sf2/sf1) of the takeoff's page. Model is paper-scale, different pages get different scales, and absolute tolerances (boolean kSnapTol 0.05, h_len < 0.001, arc radius tolerance 0.1, segment counts chord/5) act at a different relative size.
- Windows tests: tests/integration/rendering/test_page_flip_mesh.py::test_mesh_pipeline_preserves_model_scale_when_flipping; tests/integration/rendering/test_page_flip_mesh.py::PageFlip3DMeshTests::test_mesh_pipeline_preserves_model_scale_when_flipping; tests/domain/services/test_coordinate_transformation_service.py::OSTCoordinateSystemThreeDTests
- note (Phase 1): Earlier iOS log claims this matches desktop transform_to_3d(..., scale_ratio); it does not match the current Windows pipeline (see staleDocClaims). Fixing this also removes the sr = inf risk for sf2 = 0. Fix first: MSH-02 depends on it. Call sites: ProjectService.mm, ExportService.mm (sr = 1/ratio).
- outcome (fixed): ProjectService.generateMeshesIntoRenderer and ExportService use generate_view_meshes / generate_*_export: raw OST units, no division by the page scale ratio (the local Phase 1 loops and build_hole_map are deleted). Files: `Core/Meshing/TakeoffMeshBuilder.hpp, Core/Meshing/TakeoffMeshBuilder.cpp, Core/Meshing/MeshFactory.hpp, Services/ProjectService.mm, Services/ExportService.mm`.

#### 2. QTY-01: Takeoffs with a parent_uid are skipped for every condition type (attachments placed on areas count 0)
- owner: quantities | impact: wrong-numbers | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- aliases: COND-M01, LP-05
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/domain/services/condition_quantity_service.py:85-91`: primaries = takeoffs where (not condition.is_area) or parent_uid is root ('', '0', 'None'). Only AREA takeoffs with a parent are treated as holes. Non-area conditions count every takeoff even when parent_uid is set, so an attachment placed on an area contributes its own quantity (count 1).
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Domain/UomService.hpp:767-768`: 'if (t->is_hole()) continue;' is applied for every condition type, so Attachment (and any non-area) takeoffs that have a parent are dropped. Imported attachments therefore report 0. Verified: lifecycle fixture gives attachment q1=0 (Windows 1) and linear [5,7] with a parent gives 5 (Windows 12).
- Windows tests: tests/domain/services/test_condition_quantity_service.py::TakeoffLifecycleQuantityTests::test_attachment_contributes_its_own_quantity; tests/domain/services/test_condition_quantity_service.py::ConditionQuantityServiceExactTests::test_non_area_conditions_count_every_takeoff_even_with_a_parent_uid; tests/domain/services/test_condition_quantity_service.py (parent/hole cases) (+2 more, see PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json)
- note (Phase 1): Same is_hole skip exists in ProjectService.mm:167 for mesh generation (mesh area, unverified). LP-05 is the umbrella for compute_page_quantities: it also covers QTY-02, QTY-03, QTY-04 and QTY-18. COND-M01 is the same defect seen from the conditions list.
- outcome (fixed): compute_page_quantities skips a takeoff as a hole only when its condition is an AREA condition (cdn.is_area() && t->is_hole()); linear/count/attachment takeoffs with a parent_uid are counted (attachment on an area = 1, linear [5,7] with parent = 12). Parent token 'None' (QTY-18, needs-decision) deliberately left on the 2-token Takeoff::is_hole rule and not tested. Files: `Core/Domain/UomService.hpp`.

A small backtracking regex engine runs the Windows patterns verbatim (leftmost, greedy... #### 5. QTY-10: Area slope factor must use absolute rise and run (iOS uses signed values)
- owner: quantities | impact: wrong-numbers | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- aliases: COND-AREAABS-01
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/domain/services/uom_service.py:676-682,731-735`: _calc_slope_factor(rise, run, use_absolute=(condition_type == AREA)): for areas abs(rise), abs(run) are used. Linear: only strictly positive rise AND run give sqrt(rise²+run²)/run; negative values are not a slope.
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Domain/UomService.hpp:518-523,673`: Always signed: for Area, a negative rise or run gives factor 1.0. Verified: 120x120 area, rise=-3, run=4, calc 11, SF -> 100.0 (Windows 125.0); also affects perimeter calc 14/15/33, calc 17, 21, 22, 34 and 35 and the hole area/perimeter scaling.
- Windows tests: tests/domain/services/test_uom_service.py::AreaSlopeBehaviorTests::test_area_quantity_uses_absolute_slope_magnitude; tests/domain/services/test_uom_service.py::UomGeometryHelperTests::test_slope_factor_is_the_hypotenuse_over_run_and_ignores_flat_or_unset_slopes; tests/domain/services/test_uom_service.py::UomAreaQuantityTests::test_slope_scales_area_perimeter_and_hole_measures_and_uses_magnitudes (+5 more, see PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json)
- note (Phase 1): The dialog-side half (store abs() for Area rise/run) is DLG-RISE-01.
- outcome (fixed): calc_slope_factor(rise, run, use_absolute=false); calculate_condition_quantities passes use_absolute for Area so negative rise/run use their magnitudes (120x120, rise -3, run 4 -> 125 SF); Linear stays strictly-positive only. Dialog half (store abs() for Area rise/run) is DLG-RISE-01, not part of this item. Files: `Core/Domain/UomService.hpp`.

#### 7. GEO-06: ost_to_pdf_coordinates: iOS uses a stale legacy rotation/flip model; results differ for every rotation != 0
- owner: geometry | impact: wrong-numbers | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed-ui-unverified
- aliases: RPV-01, LP-03, GEO-16
- Windows: `cpp_extensions/src/coord_transform/coord_transform.cpp:52-85; cpp_extensions/src/common/page_transform.hpp:21-56`: points_per_unit=72*sf1/sf2. The point (x,y)*ppu in the UNROTATED page (top-left origin, width W, height H in pts) is flipped first (flip_x: W-x, flip_y: H-y), then rotated CCW: 90 gives (y, W-x), 180 gives (W-x, H-y), 270 gives (H-y, x). The result is returned with y' = output_height - y (Y-up PDF); output dims swap W/H for 90/270. ...
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Geometry/coord_transform.cpp:8-36,61-137; PlanView/PlanView.mm:258-268,300-306`: The legacy 14-argument function (coord_scale, is_page_rotated, auto_rotate_180, offsets) is retained. It applies flip against the rotated output size in OST units, then a reverse_rotation that is a reflection for 90/180/270. Same inputs: rot90 (-120,360),(720,360),(-120,440),(720,440); rot180 (240,80),(240,720),(360,80),(360,720); ...
- Windows tests: tests/domain/services/test_coordinate_transformation_service.py::OSTCoordinateSystemParsingTests::test_pdf_coordinates_honour_rotation_and_flips_on_a_scaled_page; tests/domain/services/test_coordinate_transformation_service.py::CanonicalCoordinateValidationTests; tests/domain/services/test_coordinate_transformation_service.py::OSTCoordinateSystemEdgeTests::test_pdf_coordinates_default_to_a_letter_width_when_flipping (+7 more, see PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json)
- note (Phase 1): Together with GEO-08, the display semantic to follow is the Windows one: OST positions live in the unrotated page space and the view applies flip then CCW rotation about the page centre (zoom_handler.py:339-347). ...
- outcome (fixed-ui-unverified): Both wiring entries done. The two 14-argument ost_to_pdf_coordinates calls (baseline compile errors) are gone: PlanView no longer calls it. Every takeoff outline now comes from Core ost_footprint::canvas_footprint (Windows TakeoffRenderer._create_path in scene px of the UNROTATED page, then ost_page_transform::transform_top_left_point per vertex), the handle points from ost_... [UI] Core transforms are bit-exact against the Windows pyd; the displayed canvas (output page, mirrored/rotated PDF, overlays) is UIKit/CoreGraphics and needs a device check. #### 9. QTY-07: perp_slope() has the dx and dy tests swapped, breaking arc centers for axis-aligned chord segments
- owner: quantities | impact: wrong-numbers | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- aliases: CURVE-01
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/domain/services/uom_service.py:369-374; cpp_extensions/src/linear_geom/linear_geom.cpp:29-35`: perp_slope(dx,dy): dy==0 -> +inf; dx==0 -> 0.0; else -dx/dy. (1,0) -> inf, (0,1) -> 0.0, (0,0) -> inf.
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Domain/UomService.hpp:323-330`: dx==0 -> +inf; dy==0 -> 0.0; else -dx/dy. (1,0) -> 0.0, (0,1) -> inf. A horizontal or vertical p1->control or control->p2 segment picks the wrong center formula, so arc length is wrong. Verified: position [1,0,0,1,1,1] curve=0 gives 1.6186 (Windows 2.1962, headline test expects pi/sqrt(2)=2.2214 +-0.03); arc (135,45,270 deg) R=100 gives 236.72 vs 392.64.
- Windows tests: tests/domain/services/test_uom_service.py::UomServiceTests::test_axis_aligned_circular_curve_uses_its_arc_length; tests/domain/services/test_uom_service.py::LinearQuantity::test_curve_helpers_agree_with_closed_forms; tests/domain/services/test_uom_service.py::UomCurvedArcTests::test_arcs_with_axis_aligned_chord_segments_use_the_dedicated_center_formulas (+2 more, see PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json)
- note (Phase 1): (test id for helper test is UomServiceTests... under LinearQuantity: class UomLinearQuantityTests::test_curve_helpers_agree_with_closed_forms). Fixing perp_slope alone is not enough: the direction (QTY-08) and at-end (QTY-09) rules must also change. ...
- outcome (fixed): perp_slope tests dy == 0 first (+inf) then dx == 0 (0.0), as Windows. With QTY-08/QTY-09 the axis-aligned arcs now match Windows (100000 random arcs differ by < 1e-12). Files: `Core/Domain/UomService.hpp`.

- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Domain/UomService.hpp:451-461`: clockwise = (cross1+cross2) < 0, then end_angle is shifted. This picks the minor arc when the control point is near one end of a >180 degree arc. Verified: (0,250,251) R=100 gives 190.21 (Windows 437.74); (0,110,100) gives 174.51 (Windows 453.43); 22% of random 3-point arcs differ (1314/6000), e.g. [2.806,-0.005,3.249,-0.853,-4.437,9.953] 274.98 vs 0.96. ...

- note (Phase 1): CURVE-02 also covers the straight-chord rule (QTY-09).
- outcome (fixed): Arc sweep is now ccw_to_end = (end-start) mod 2pi, ccw_to_control = (control-start) mod 2pi, angle_diff = ccw_to_end if ccw_to_control <= ccw_to_end else ccw_to_end - 2pi (Python floored modulo via floor_mod). Also replaced std::hypot by accurate_hypot (matches CPython math.hypot bit for bit on 55013 samples; std::hypot differs in ~17%) because the huge-circle guard |r1-r2| ... Pinned by: Dbp05_TakeoffIncrementsZero.ZeroKeepsSnappingDisabledUntilTheOwnerDecides; Dbp04_TakeoffIncrements.NullLoadsAsOneInch; Dbp04_TakeoffIncrements.SnapIncrementFollowsTheMeasurementSystem.
- possible Windows defect: ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/hierarchy_reader.py:126; ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/viewer_sync_coordinator.py:233; ost_visualizer/domain/services/project_data_service.py:276; ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/view.py:3955-3962; ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/view.py:470; ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/components/drag_handler.py:261-264; ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/components/placement_mode.py:766-774; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/settings_reader.py:151; ost_visualizer/presentation/dialogs/cover_sheet/dialog.py:880-886; ost_visualizer/presentation/dialogs/cover_sheet/dialog.py:2385-2388; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/settings_operations.py:178; tests/presentation/components/plan_view/test_view.py:14859-14876: Two Windows readers disagree on the same stored value. The plan-view loader turns a stored 0 into 1.0 (`x or 1.0`), while the Cover Sheet reader and dialog keep 0. The plan view is written to support an increment <= 0 as 'snapping off' and a Windows test pins that (test_view.py:14859-14876), but through the database that branch can only be reached by a negative value, never by a stored 0. Scenario: Cover Sheet, 'Takeoff in Increments of:' = 0 (user wants no snapping), Save. Reopening the Cover Sheet shows 0, but the plan view of the same bid loads takeoff_increments = 1.0 through hierarchy_reader.py:126, so every placement and drag still snaps to 1 inch and 'off' is unreachable. Severity: inconsistent, confidence: medium. Suggested Windows fix: Pick one meaning. Either keep a stored 0 in hierarchy_reader.py:126 (`1.0 if bid_row.TakeoffIncrements is None else float(bid_row.TakeoffIncrements)`) so 0 = snapping off as view.py:3955 already supports, or reject values <= 0 in the Cover Sheet (dialog.py:2386) and use the same fallback in settings_reader.py:151.

#### 25. GEO-05: Area slope: iOS applies a descending slope for opposite rise/run signs and uses the wrong rotation sense for its mirrored frame
- owner: meshing | impact: wrong-numbers | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- aliases: MSH-03, MSH-04
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/core/geometry/area.py:40-55; tests/presentation/visualization/core/geometry/test_area.py`: has_slope = run != 0 (rise/run truthy). proj = (x-cx)*cos(-rot)+(y-cy)*sin(-rot) in the Windows 3D frame (X=-x, Y=y). z_top = z_offset + thickness + span*prog*abs(rise)/abs(run), where prog=(proj-min)/span (span>0). The slope always rises along +proj. ...
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Meshing/gen_area.cpp:22-33,47,52-79`: asc = (rise>0&&run>0)||(rise<0&&run<0); if descending y = base + thickness + slope_h - h_chg. So rise=-1,run=4 gives a mirrored top, unlike Windows. proj uses rot=-rotation in the iOS (x,z)=(-x,-y) frame, i.e. X*cos(r)-Z*sin(r), whereas Windows is X*cos(r)+Z*sin(r) after Z=-Y. Rotation != 0 therefore slopes in a mirrored direction. ...
- Windows tests: tests/presentation/visualization/core/geometry/test_area.py::AreaSlopeBehaviorTests::test_area_mesh_slope_direction_uses_rotation_not_rise_run_signs; tests/presentation/visualization/core/geometry/test_area.py::test_area_mesh_slope_direction_uses_rotation_not_rise_run_signs
- note (Phase 1): Verified by running both through harnesses. PORTING_LOG.md:1492-1497 ('Ascending/descending logic matches desktop') is wrong for the current Windows code. The linear-mesh slope (up/down by sign) is a different Windows code path and is left to the meshing mapper. ...
- outcome (fixed): gen_area.cpp: the slope always rises along +proj with z_top = z_offset + thickness + span*prog*(|rise|/|run|) (the sign of rise/run is ignored, descending branch removed); proj = (x-cx)*cos(-rot) - (z-cz)*sin(-rot) in the iOS frame, which is the Windows (x-cx)*cos + (Y-cy)*sin with Y = -z (rotation sense fixed); span > 0 instead of 1e-10. Ported test_area.py plus 17 Windows ... Files: `Core/Meshing/gen_area.cpp`.

#### 40. ELV-02: Metric elevations: compound m+cm without 'and'/'+' dropped, '+' glued form fails, decimal comma, 'centimeters', signs, word boundaries
- owner: parsers-units | impact: wrong-numbers | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- Windows: `position_parser.py:86-105 (_parse_metric_elevation), 108-129; elevation_text.py:10-18`: Accepted shapes: '[-]N[.|,]N m [and|+] [-]N[.|,]N cm' (the connector is OPTIONAL), 'N m|meter|meters', 'N cm|centimeter|centimeters', case-insensitive, decimal comma = point, unit must end at a word boundary ('5cmx' invalid). A leading minus on the metres part negates metres+cm together; a minus on cm alone subtracts. Result cm/2.54 inches.
- iOS (as mapped): `ProjectStore.cpp:376-404`: Number scanner accepts digits and '.' only (no comma). Only the first number is used unless the text after the unit starts with 'and' or '+' separated by a space: "Wall @T 1m 20cm" -> 100 cm only (39.37 in; Windows 47.244 in), "1.5m 20cm" -> 150 cm, "3m+20cm" -> 0 (rest_low[1]=='+' fails the 'm' test at :384), "2,5 m" -> 0, "2 CENTIMETERS"/"2 centimeter" -> ...
- Windows tests: tests/infrastructure/parsers/test_position_parser.py::MetricElevationParsingTests; ::MetricSpellingBoundaryTests; ::MetricSignAppliesToTheWholeValueTests (+2 more, see PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json)
- outcome (fixed): Metric grammar ported (optional and/+ connector, glued +, decimal comma, centimeter(s)/meter(s), case-insensitive, word boundary after the unit, sign of metres applies to metres+cm, minus on cm subtracts). Product and sum are separate statements so an FMA-contracting compiler cannot change the IEEE rounding. Files: `Core/Domain/PositionParser.cpp`.

#### 41. ELV-03: Elevation marker detection differs (first '@' vs last ' @T'/' @B' / ' @ ' / ' @'; leading space; case)
- owner: parsers-units | impact: wrong-numbers | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/domain/services/elevation.py:25-59 (parse_elevation, used by position_parser.py:112)`: Marker must be preceded by a space; the LAST ' @T' or ' @B' wins; if none, the last ' @ ' or ' @' is a legacy bare marker (bottom). T/B are case-sensitive: ' @t' falls to the bare-marker rule and its value 't 5...' is not an elevation. Text with '@' not preceded by a space ('Wall@T 5' 0"', '@T 6"', 'a@b @T ...') is not a marker.
- iOS (as mapped): `ProjectStore.cpp:296-314`: Uses name.find('@') (FIRST '@' anywhere), then optionally eats a 't'/'T'/'b'/'B', case-insensitive. 'Wall @T 5' 6" @B 3' 0"' -> (66, top) (Windows (36, bottom)); 'Wall@T 5' 0"' -> (60, top) (Windows none); '@T 6"' -> (6, top) (Windows none); 'Wall @t 5' 0"' -> (60, top) (Windows none); ...
- Windows tests: tests/infrastructure/parsers/test_position_parser.py::ElevationValueBoundaryTests::test_the_last_elevation_marker_wins_and_a_legacy_bare_marker_is_a_bottom; ::CompoundMetricConversionAndEmptyBaseNameTests::test_a_marker_without_the_leading_space_is_not_an_elevation_marker; ::test_an_elevation_marker_with_an_empty_base_name_still_has_an_elevation
- note (Phase 1): parseElevation in EditConditionHelpers.mm already implements the correct marker rules; extract_z_value should reuse that logic (moved into Core).
- outcome (fixed): parse_elevation ported (rfind of ' @T'/' @B', last explicit marker wins, legacy ' @ '/' @' = bottom, one separating ASCII space dropped, T/B case-sensitive, marker needs the leading space). EditConditionHelpers.mm::parseElevation (Controllers, not touched) could later be replaced by ost_domain::parse_elevation (optional). Files: `Core/Domain/PositionParser.cpp`.

- outcome (fixed): inchesToDisplay(inches, metric) is Core inches_to_display; every caller passes _metric; parseFraction / parseDimField / displayToInches are deleted. #### 53. GEO-07: signed_area is un-centred; loses precision at large offsets
- owner: geometry | impact: wrong-numbers | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- aliases: RPV-11
- Windows: `cpp_extensions/src/geom_utils/geom_utils.cpp:106-119`: Computes the 2x shoelace sum relative to the first vertex ((x_i-x_0)*(y_j-y_0) - (x_j-x_0)*(y_i-y_0)), so it is translation-invariant. n=pos.size()/2; returns 0 for n<2. The concave test polygon translated by 1e8 returns exactly 12 and -12 reversed.
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Geometry/geom_utils.cpp:61-74`: Plain un-centred sum of x_i*y_j - x_j*y_i. At an offset of 1e8 the same polygon returns 16 / -16 instead of 12 / -12 (verified in the harness), and a unit square at 1e8 returns 0.0 (Windows 2.0). It is used for the winding sign in DragController.mm:93,101.
- Windows tests: tests/presentation/components/plan_view/components/test_geometry_utils.py::TakeoffLifecyclePrecisionTests::test_native_winding_and_concave_rotation_pivot_preserve_translation
- note (Phase 1): For realistic page coordinates (about 1e4) the sign is almost always right; the wrong magnitude is still a pinned divergence. The mesh code has its own un-centred calc_signed_area (gen_area.cpp:10-20), the same as Windows area.py:7-15 (also un-centred, /2), so that one matches.
- outcome (fixed): ost_geom::signed_area now sums relative to the first vertex exactly like Windows geom_utils.cpp (translation invariant). L-shape at +1e8 gives exactly 12 / -12, unit square at 1e8 gives 2.0 (was 16/-16 and 0.0). 400000-case bit-exact differential run against the Windows C++ source (signed_area bits identical). Callers (DragController.mm:93,101) need no change. Files: `Core/Geometry/geom_utils.cpp`.

#### 54. QTY-14: Polygon area is taken about (0,0) instead of the first vertex (precision loss for far-from-origin polygons)

Real OST coordinates are page inches (rarely above 1e4), so practical error is far below 1e-6 sq in.
- Windows tests: tests/domain/services/test_uom_service.py::UomGeometryHelperTests::test_polygon_area_is_absolute_orientation_free_and_needs_three_vertices; tests/domain/services/test_uom_service.py::UomWaveThreeReworkTests::test_polygon_area_of_a_small_polygon_a_billion_inches_from_the_origin; tests/domain/services/test_uom_service.py::AreaSlopeBehaviorTests::test_area_quantity_retains_fractional_origin_precision (+1 more, see PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json)

#### 68. DBP-11: SQL errors are swallowed everywhere: statements keep going after a failure, the transaction commits, and every writer returns true
- owner: database-persistence | impact: data-safety | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- aliases: BRW-07, COND-M04
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/mdb_writer.py:63-105 (_connection: commit only if no error, rollback and re-raise otherwise; nested depth tracking)`: Any error rolls the whole operation back and the write method returns False (or raises under the strict policy).
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Persistence/OstDatabase.cpp:56-62 (step returns rc==SQLITE_ROW only), 98-114 (no busy_timeout), 125-134; e.g. ...`: Failures of INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE are invisible. Host repro (fail_inject.py): trigger raising ABORT on DELETE FROM BidPages, then permanently_delete_project -> rc=1, Bids row deleted, BidPages row still present, all earlier cascade deletes committed. duplicate_condition returns the new uid even if the INSERT failed; import_project ignores commit().
- Windows tests: tests/integration/mdb/test_ownership_and_atomicity.py:154 (test_later_chunk_delete_failure_rolls_back_page_condition_and_folder_batches); tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_project_operations.py:902; tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_takeoff_operations.py:1074,2344 (+4 more, see PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json)
- note (Phase 1): Root cause of several other silent-success behaviors (DBP-10, DBP-12..17). Root cause: Statement::step() returns rc == SQLITE_ROW only, so DONE and error are indistinguishable. Fix in OstDatabase then make every writer check results and roll back.
- outcome (fixed): A failed update (dialog alert), a failed duplicate ('Duplicate Error') and a failed delete ('Delete Error') are shown instead of ignored. iOS-only texts: failure dialogs keep the Windows hint "The database may be locked by another application."; "New Project / Failed to create project." and "Save Failed / Failed to save the display settings." are iOS wording; alert flows need a device check. Files: `Core/Persistence/OstDatabase.hpp, Core/Persistence/OstDatabase.cpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore.cpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore_Internal.hpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Conditions.cpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Folders.cpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Import.cpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Takeoffs.cpp, Services/ImportService.mm, Controllers/DatabaseViewController.mm (+2 more)`.

#### 81. UND-02: Undo/redo advances history even when the persistence write fails
- owner: settings-undo-camera | impact: data-safety | disposition: fix | testable: needs-extraction-from-objc | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/presentation/services/undo_redo_service.py:469-524 (_submit_history_transition: the entry moves stacks only on COMMITTED), :197-231 (push_local: action Fal ...`: If the undo or redo action returns False or raises, the entry stays on its source stack and is retried by the next undo or redo. A failed forward persistence pushes no history entry.
- iOS (as mapped): `Controllers/ViewController/Components/UndoRedoController.mm:47-93; Controllers/ViewController/ViewController.mm:739-776 (_applyPosition, _removeTakeoffFromProject and _ad ...`: performUndo and performRedo always pop and push the opposite stack and call undoRedoDidModify. In-memory state and history advance although SQLite saveTakeoffPosition, deleteTakeoff or insertTakeoff may have failed. An insert failure leaves uid empty but still appends the takeoff to the project.
- Windows tests: tests/presentation/services/test_undo_redo_service.py:86-125,584-638
- note (Phase 1): Needs the delegate protocol to return success; the persistence helpers already return BOOL or nil.
- outcome (fixed): An entry only changes stacks when its undo/redo action reports success (UndoHistory::undo/redo: false or an exception leaves it where it was, the next undo retries it; Windows _submit_history_transition). Every UndoRedoDelegate apply method now returns BOOL (applyPosition, applyPositions [UND-07 handover], applyNegatives, removeTakeoffs, addTakeoffs). ViewController stores f... Windows insert_takeoffs validates parents against rows that exist BEFORE the batch; the iOS batch accepts a parent restored earlier in the same step (uid-retaining restore); documented and tested. DECIDED 2026-11-19 (keep, no code change): the batch insert of takeoffs validates in list order (a parent restored earlier in the same step counts). Files: `Core/Domain/UndoHistory.hpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore.hpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Takeoffs.cpp, Services/ImportService.h, Services/ImportService.mm, Controllers/ViewController/Components/UndoRedoController.h, Controllers/ViewController/Components/UndoRedoController.mm, Controllers/ViewController/ViewController.mm`.
- decision (2026-11-19): keep iOS: The batch insert of takeoffs validates in list order: a parent restored earlier in the same undo / redo step counts (Windows validates against the rows that exist before the batch). No code change. Pinned by: Und02_InsertTakeoffs.ASetIsRestoredWithItsParentLinks; Und02_InsertTakeoffs.ARefusedTakeoffRollsBackTheOnesBeforeIt; Und02_InsertTakeoffs.AParentOnAnotherPageRefusesTheBatch.

- note (Phase 1): Requires an update API that takes a field mask or a before/after diff. Needs a field-mask or before/after diff API and a real dirty flag (form state != saved state).
- outcome (fixed): The delegate is - (BOOL)didSaveCondition:changes:; _applyChanges computes stored / condition_to_save / collect_condition_changes, an empty set is a success without a write, only after YES the dialog shifts its map (shift_conflicting_ref_nos) and stores; the VC calls updateConditionWithUID:inBid:changes: first, then project.shift_conflicting_ref_nos and stores; _shiftConflict... NULL stored RefNo: Windows condition_operations.py:606 raises on it (cannot take a new number); iOS treats it as not the new number and the update goes on. DECIDED 2026-11-19 (keep iOS, no code change; aliases DLG-SAVE-01, COND-M06 follow): possible Windows defect (condition_operations.py:606 `int(current_row[0])` raises TypeError on a NULL RefNo, so update_condition returns False). Test gap: no host test stores a NULL RefNo. Files: `Core/Domain/ConditionChanges.hpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore.hpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Conditions.cpp, Core/Data/ProjectData.hpp, Services/ImportService.h, Services/ImportService.mm`.
- decision (2026-11-19): keep iOS: A NULL stored RefNo counts as 'not the new number' and the condition update goes on (Windows: int(None) raises, update_condition returns False). No code change. Covers the aliases DLG-SAVE-01 and COND-M06. Test gap: No host test stores a NULL RefNo and updates its number (grep for RefNo = NULL in Config/Tests finds nothing). The neighbouring shift tests are ConditionUpdate.TakingATakenReferenceNumberShiftsOnlyTheConflictingPeers and ConditionUpdate.KeepingTheSameNumberOrTakingAFreeOneShiftsNothing (test_condition_update.cpp).
- possible Windows defect: ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/condition_operations.py:591-607; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/condition_operations.py:673-700; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/condition_operations.py:734; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/condition_operations.py:49-50; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/bid_data_reader.py:920; ost_visualizer/presentation/dialogs/edit_condition_dialog.py:850; ost_visualizer/presentation/dialogs/edit_condition_dialog.py:1449-1469; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/database_creator.py:165; tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_bid_data_reader.py:3908: Windows accepts a NULL BidConditions.RefNo in the schema, the loader (reads it as 0, pinned by a test) and renumber_conditions (int(max_ref_no or 0)), but the update path int(current_row[0]) raises TypeError, so update_condition returns False. In the dialog such a condition shows Cond. No. '0', and since ref_no < 1 is refused the user must type a number, which then makes the write fail: the condition can never be saved with a number from the dialog. Scenario: Foreign OST data with BidConditions.RefNo NULL: open the condition, the Cond. No. field shows 0, Save is refused ('must be a positive whole number'); type 5 and Save: update_condition returns False and nothing is saved. Severity: inconsistent, confidence: medium. Suggested Windows fix: In _shift_conflicting_ref_nos_for_update use int(current_row[0] or 0) (treat NULL as 0, like the loader and renumber_conditions). Add a test with RefNo NULL.

#### 89. RPV-M01: Area edge-handle drag is not validated (self-touching or winding-reversed polygons are committed)
- owner: rendering-planview | impact: data-safety | disposition: fix | testable: needs-extraction-from-objc | verdict: CONFIRMED (verifier-added) | status: fixed
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/components/drag_handler.py:89-99, 481-506; input_handler.py:1434-1439`: For any area vertex drag (handle_index >= 0, which includes edge handles) the proposed position must pass polygon_is_valid and must not reverse winding versus the last valid position. Otherwise the previous valid geometry is kept, and on release the last valid position is what gets committed.
- iOS (as mapped): `Controllers/ViewController/Components/DragController.mm:53-69 (edge branch), 91-107 (validation only in the corner branch)`: The edge branch writes both corners with no polygon_is_valid or winding check, so an edge dragged across the opposite edge is persisted as an inverted polygon. Only corner drags are validated.
- Windows tests: tests/presentation/components/plan_view/components/test_drag_handler.py::test_area_invalid_resize_keeps_previous_valid_geometry; tests/presentation/components/plan_view/components/test_drag_handler.py::test_area_resize_that_reverses_winding_keeps_previous_valid_geometry; tests/presentation/components/plan_view/components/test_drag_handler.py::test_polygon_edit_requires_three_valid_points_and_stable_winding
- note (Phase 1): Ran Windows _polygon_edit_geometry_valid with a harness. Shares the validity function with RPV-14; once polygon_is_valid is ported, the edge branch just needs the same check the corner branch already has.
- outcome (fixed): Core ost_drag::polygon_edit_geometry_valid + drag_candidate_valid: every area vertex OR edge drag (handle >= 0, >= 6 values) must give a valid polygon with the same winding as the last valid geometry, otherwise the step is rejected and the position stays (DragController asks the delegate before applying the candidate; ViewController builds the HierarchyModel and calls Core). Same scene-vs-OST rounding caveat as GEO-M02 for degenerate polygons. Files: `Core/Geometry/drag_rules.hpp, Core/Geometry/drag_rules.cpp, Controllers/ViewController/Components/DragController.h, Controllers/ViewController/Components/DragController.mm, Controllers/ViewController/ViewController.mm`.

#### 90. IMP-12: OSP image staging overwrites/collides: basename-only destination, no content check
- owner: import-ost-osp | impact: data-safety | disposition: fix | testable: needs-extraction-from-objc | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/importers/osp_importer.py:310-347,400-446`: Each referenced packaged image goes to <working>/<ost stem>/<name> if its member is directly under TempImages!.tmp, otherwise <ost stem>/Images/<sha1(member.casefold())[:16]>/<name>. If the destination exists with different content the file is written to <stem>-<sha256[:16|32|full]><suffix>; identical content is reused; ...
- iOS (as mapped): `Services/ImportService.mm:172-218`: Every .pdf entry anywhere in the CAB is written to Documents/OST/<ost stem>/<basename> with atomically:YES, overwriting any existing file. Two members with the same basename in different folders overwrite each other (and both references are then rewritten to the same path). ...
- Windows tests: tests/infrastructure/mdb/importers/test_osp_importer.py::test_osp_import_does_not_overwrite_different_existing_image; tests/infrastructure/mdb/importers/test_osp_importer.py::test_osp_import_reuses_identical_existing_image; tests/infrastructure/mdb/importers/test_osp_importer.py::test_osp_import_resolves_most_specific_nested_member_without_overwrite (+3 more, see PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json)
- note (Phase 1): The iOS root (Documents/OST/<stem>) is the platform-adapted equivalent of get_default_working_dir()/<stem>; only the collision and staging logic is the divergence.
- outcome (fixed): OspPackage.cpp ports _image_destination_relative_path (flat name or Images/<sha1(casefold member)[:16]>/<name>, reserved device names refused), _non_conflicting_image_destination (existing different content is never overwritten: <stem>-<sha256[:16|32|full]><suffix>, identical content reused, full collision error), _copy_referenced_images (every member must be an extracted fi... A destination that is a directory is treated as different content (Windows compares directory size with file size, accidental); pinned by test_staging_names_taken_by_directories_are_never_reused. DECIDED 2026-11-19 (keep, no code change): a staging destination that is a directory counts as different content. Possible Windows defect: osp_importer.py:413-425 and :428-438 compare a directory size with a file size (an equal size would raise). Files: `Services/ImportService.mm`.
- decision (2026-11-19): keep iOS: A staging destination that is a directory counts as different content (a non-regular destination is never reused); Windows compares a directory size with a file size. No code change. Pinned by: Imp12_NonConflicting.ANameTakenByADirectoryIsNeverReused.
- possible Windows defect: ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/importers/osp_importer.py:413-425; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/importers/osp_importer.py:428-438; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/importers/osp_importer.py:333; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/importers/osp_importer.py:217-219; tests/infrastructure/mdb/importers/test_osp_importer.py:1933-1943: The Windows test says a staging name taken by a directory is never reused, but the code only meets that by luck: a directory is compared by st_size with the incoming file, and when the sizes are equal the code opens the directory and raises PermissionError instead of choosing the digest-suffixed name. On NTFS a directory reports size 0, so on Windows only an empty (0-byte) image member triggers it; reachable only with a crafted OSP whose member names make a file and a directory collide. The import then fails closed with a generic error. Scenario: OSP package where one member's staging destination is an existing directory and the referenced image is empty: the import dies with PermissionError (reported as a failed import) instead of staging the image under a suffixed name. Severity: cosmetic, confidence: low. Suggested Windows fix: In _files_have_same_content (osp_importer.py:428) start with `if not (first.is_file() and second.is_file()): return False`, or test dest_path.is_file() at :414 and :421.

#### 91. IMP-13: CAB reader silently skips corrupt blocks, ignores checksums and treats PDF extraction failure as success
- owner: import-ost-osp | impact: data-safety | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- Windows: `cpp_extensions/src/cab/cab_compressor.cpp:380-423,495-520; osp_importer.py:243-247`: FDICopy extracts through the Windows cabinet library. Any decode or corruption error makes extract_cab return false, the import fails with 'the OSP archive could not be extracted', and nothing is imported. The legacy test fixtures write CFDATA checksum 0 (meaning none).
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Import/CabExtractor.cpp:176-258 (blocks with missing 'CK' or an inflate error are skipped with continue; csum at offset 0 never read); ...`: A bad MSZIP block is dropped without error, so every later byte in that folder shifts. extract_cab_file only fails if the resulting buffer is shorter than the file's end; otherwise it returns shifted, wrong bytes. There is no check that produced bytes equal cbUncomp. CFDATA checksums are not verified. Stored-block truncation just breaks the loop. ...
- Windows tests: tests/integration/native/test_osp_cab.py; tests/infrastructure/mdb/importers/test_osp_importer.py::test_osp_import_extraction_failure_fails_import_and_cleans_temp_files; tests/infrastructure/mdb/importers/test_osp_importer.py::test_extraction_failure_message_names_the_cause_and_archive
- note (Phase 1): Windows FDI behavior on bad checksums is the documented library behavior (I could not run FDI here). The 'fail on corruption' rule is clear from the Windows tests (extract_cab false means the import fails). A host test needs a zlib on the build machine.
- outcome (fixed): importOSPFile no longer extracts per member and ignores failures: stage_osp_package uses the single streaming extraction, so any corrupt block / checksum / short member fails the import ('The OSP archive could not be extracted') before anything is staged or imported. Files: `Core/Import/CabExtractor.hpp, Core/Import/CabExtractor.cpp, Services/ImportService.mm`.

#### 98. EXP-09: Export filename: iOS uses the raw bid name only; Windows builds '<bid> - <pages>.<ext>' with sanitising and a 255-character limit
- owner: export-formats | impact: data-safety | disposition: fix | testable: needs-extraction-from-objc | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- Windows: `infrastructure/visualization_provider.py:55-89; infrastructure/utils/filename.py:3-6; application/services/export_service.py:50-65,94-99`: sanitize_filename replaces [\x00-\x1f\\/:"*?<>|] with '_'. 1 page gives '<bid> - <page>.<ext>'; 2 pages '<bid> - A + B.<ext>'; 3 or more '<bid> - A + B + N more.<ext>' (N = count-2). Length is capped at 255 by truncating the bid to '...' form, else the page, else 'Export_<N>_pages.<ext>'. ...
- iOS (as mapped): `Services/ExportService.mm:94-98,117-120,159-161,188-190`: The file name is bid_name + extension, with no sanitising, no page names and no length limit. A bid name containing '/' becomes a sub-path that does not exist, so the write fails and the user sees 'No visible meshes to export' (see EXP-21). An empty bid name gives 'export'.
- Windows tests: tests/infrastructure/test_visualization_provider.py::ExportFilenameTests::test_suggested_export_filename_replaces_windows_control_characters; tests/infrastructure/test_visualization_provider.py::ExportFilenameCompatibilityTests::test_export_filename_consolidation_preserves_html_and_mesh_policy; tests/infrastructure/test_visualization_provider.py::ExportFilenameCompatibilityTests::test_export_filename_consolidation_preserves_long_name_fallbacks (+1 more, see PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json)

- outcome (fixed): The export file is named '<bid> - <pages>.<ext>' like Windows _prepare_export_filename: sanitize_filename ([\x00-\x1f\\/:"*?<>|] -> '_'), 1 / 2 / 3+ page forms ('A + B + N more'), 255 code point limit with the bid '...' cut (> 10 characters left), else the page cut, else Export_<N>_pages. New Core ost_geometry::export_text::sanitize_filename / export_filename (Core/Export/ex... Page names of multi-page exports follow the page-selector order, not the Windows click order (the iOS selection is a std::set); identical for one page; covered by the user decision EXP-18 (keep sorted order). A name longer than 255 BYTES (multi-byte) can still fail as a write error. DECIDED 2026-11-19 (keep, no code change): exports use the sorted page order of the iOS selection (EXP-18 stays). Files: `Core/Export/export_filename.hpp, Core/Export/export_filename.cpp, Core/Meshing/ExportMeshSet.hpp, Core/Meshing/ExportMeshSet.cpp, Core/Meshing/TakeoffMeshBuilder.hpp, Core/Meshing/TakeoffMeshBuilder.cpp, Services/ExportService.mm, Services/ExportService.h`.
- decision (2026-11-19): keep iOS: Exports use the sorted page order of the iOS selection (page names, file name and output order follow the page selector, not the Windows click order); EXP-18 stays. No code change. Pinned by: ExportSelection.OnlyPagesWithTakeoffsNameTheFile; ExportSelection.OnlyTheSelectedPagesAreExported.

#### 102. COND-ELEV-01: Elevation text is reformatted when the field loses focus
- owner: conditions | impact: data-safety | disposition: fix | testable: needs-extraction-from-objc | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed-ui-unverified
- aliases: DLG-ELEV-01
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/presentation/dialogs/edit_condition_dialog.py:161-175,382,1415-1431`: The elevation field is a plain QLineEdit (only _DimensionLineEdit fields normalize on focus-out), so the typed text is kept verbatim and becomes the name suffix: base + ' @T ' / ' @B ' + typed text. z_value is parsed from it with display_to_inches.
- iOS (as mapped): `EditConditionViewController.mm:805-827 (kTagElevVal is in the isDimensionField set)`: On blur a parseable elevation is rewritten to the canonical eighths format, e.g. "10'6" becomes "10' 6\"" and "10' 6.3\"" becomes "10' 6 1/4\"", so the saved name suffix and z differ from Windows.
- Windows tests: tests/domain/services/test_elevation.py; tests/presentation/dialogs/test_edit_condition_dialog.py::test_edit_condition_rejects_non_finite_numeric_values
- outcome (fixed-ui-unverified): kTagElevVal is no longer in the focus-out normalisation set (OSTIsDimensionTag): the elevation text is kept as typed and becomes the name suffix; the text is still validated with Core parse_elevation_z (display_to_inches, metric aware, blank = 0.0, nan / inf refused with 'is not a valid elevation.'). The Elevation cell shows the cached text so a reload does not drop an unsaved elevation. DECIDED 2026-11-19 (code change, deliberate deviation): after a Save, is_top and z_value are derived from the condition NAME with extract_z_value_from_name (ost_domain::derive_elevation_from_name; an unreadable name gives 0.0 / false), so memory equals a reload; condition_to_save no longer copies form.is_top / z_value and the loader helper ost_store::extract_z_value wraps the same function. Windows reloads the conditions after every save, so this matches its effective behavior; possible Windows defect: the dialog accepts elevation text (edit_condition_dialog.py:383-385, :1415-1431) that the loader reads differently or not at all (position_parser.py:109-129). Tests: test_condition_elevation.cpp (CondElev01_*, including a round trip through the real store, 261 names; 6 of the 7 new tests failed first). Review: CONFIRMED-FIXED. Unverified: the dialog / save / 3D redraw path on a device. Files: `Core/Domain/PositionParser.hpp, Core/Domain/PositionParser.cpp, Core/Domain/ConditionStyleChange.cpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore.cpp, Controllers/EditConditionViewController/EditConditionViewController.mm, Config/Tests/Conditions/test_condition_elevation.cpp`.
- decision (2026-11-19): code change, deliberate deviation from the literal Windows behavior: After a Save, is_top and z_value are derived from the condition name with extract_z_value_from_name (ost_domain::derive_elevation_from_name; an unreadable name gives 0.0 / false), so the in-memory condition equals what a reload gives. Deliberate deviation from the dialog's own values; it matches the effective Windows behavior, which reloads the conditions from the database after every save. Pinned by: CondElev01_SaveDerivesFromName.TheNamedCasesGiveTheWindowsReload; CondElev01_SaveDerivesFromName.TopSegmentWithAnEmptyElevationIsNotTop; CondElev01_SaveDerivesFromName.StaleStoredValuesAreReplacedAndNoFieldIsWritten; CondElev01_SaveDerivesFromName.AStyleChangeDerivesFromTheNameToo; CondElev01_SaveDerivesFromName.EveryOracleRowGivesTheWindowsReload; CondElev01_SharedDerivation.TheLoaderAndTheSaveGiveTheSameValueOnEveryRow; CondElev01_SharedDerivation.AnUnreadableNameIsZeroAndNotTopWhateverTheOutputsHeld; CondElev01_ReloadParity.TheSavedInMemoryConditionEqualsTheReloadedOne.
- code change (2026-11-19): files: Core/Domain/PositionParser.hpp; Core/Domain/PositionParser.cpp; Core/Domain/ConditionStyleChange.cpp; Core/Domain/ConditionStyleChange.hpp; Core/Domain/ConditionFormRules.hpp (comment); Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore.cpp (extract_z_value now wraps the shared function); Controllers/EditConditionViewController/EditConditionViewController.mm; Config/Tests/Conditions/test_condition_elevation.cpp (new); Config/Tests/Conditions/test_condition_form.cpp (comment only). Tests added: CondElev01_SaveDerivesFromName.* (5); CondElev01_SharedDerivation.* (2); CondElev01_ReloadParity.TheSavedInMemoryConditionEqualsTheReloadedOne. Failing first: Six of the seven new tests failed on assertions before the change (for example the oracle-row check counted 3559 mismatches instead of 0; the round trip through the real store: 244 of 261 conditions differed between memory and reload). The seventh calls the new function and could not exist before. (The test file has 8 new tests now, 5 + 2 + 1; the first-run record names seven.) Review: CONFIRMED-FIXED. No defect found, no edits. Windows evidence re-read, including that Windows reloads after a save (project_write_service.py:1284-1340, :1160-1205). Not verified: The dialog / save / 3D redraw path was not run on a device (fixed-ui-unverified). Elevation text the loader does not parse reloads as (0.0, false), for example a metric "3200" without a unit and decimal inches such as "10' 6.3\"".
- possible Windows defect: ost_visualizer/presentation/dialogs/edit_condition_dialog.py:383-385; ost_visualizer/presentation/dialogs/edit_condition_dialog.py:1415-1431; ost_visualizer/domain/services/dimension_format_service.py:100-105; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/parsers/position_parser.py:109-129; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/parsers/position_parser.py:132-136; ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/meshing/mesh_factory.py:60-63; tests/infrastructure/parsers/test_position_parser.py:50-72: The dialog accepts and stores elevation texts that the loader reads differently or not at all. Since Windows reloads after the save, the dialog's z_value is discarded and the loader result decides the 3D position: a metric elevation typed as the placeholder suggests (3200) is lost on reload. Scenario: Metric bid, Top, elevation '3200' as the placeholder suggests: the dialog saves the name 'Wall @T 3200'; after the reload the 3D mesh sits at z 0 and is_top is False. Severity: wrong-number, confidence: high. Suggested Windows fix: In the dialog validate the elevation text with the loader grammar (elevation_text.is_elevation_text) and convert a bare metric number to '<n> cm' (or the imperial form) before building the name, and take z_value from extract_z_value_from_name(name) so dialog and reload agree.

Deleting a non-root with its own children, or any grandchild level, leaves descendants orphaned. The DB delete cascade has the same one-level, root-only rule.
- Windows tests: tests/domain/services/test_takeoff_domain_service.py:159; tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_takeoff_operations.py:215; tests/domain/services/test_takeoff_domain_service.py::TakeoffDescendantExpansionTests::test_selection_expands_to_every_descendant_but_not_siblings_or_ancestors

- outcome (fixed): deleteSelection:fromTakeoffs: uses ost_domain::collect_takeoffs_for_deletion(takeoffs, {selectedUID}) (all transitive descendants, parents first); the root/direct-children loop is deleted. The removal and the restore use that order and the whole set is one transaction (UND-02). Core tests of the set: test_takeoff_descendants.cpp. Files: `Core/Domain/TakeoffDeletion.hpp, Controllers/ViewController/Components/UndoRedoController.mm`.
