#### 108. GEO-M02: No hole and attachment containment validation when editing a parent area
- owner: rendering-planview | impact: data-safety | disposition: fix | testable: needs-extraction-from-objc | verdict: CONFIRMED (verifier-added) | status: fixed
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/components/drag_handler.py:485-492 (area vertex drag), 907-1035 (_attachment_position_valid, _takeoff_children_valid_for_ ...`: A parent area edit (vertex or edge drag, whole-shape or group move, rotate) is rejected when any hole child would leave the parent, a hole would intersect a sibling hole, or an attachment would not fit the area minus holes. The previous valid geometry is kept.
- iOS (as mapped): `Controllers/ViewController/Components/DragController.mm:91-111 (only polygon_is_valid plus winding guard)`: Dragging a parent area vertex or edge accepts any simple polygon, even if it cuts through existing holes or leaves attachments outside. No containment or sibling-overlap check exists.
- Windows tests: tests/presentation/components/plan_view/components/test_geometry_utils.py; tests/integration/geometry/test_attachment_validation.py
- note (Phase 1): The mapper listed the Windows geometry_utils helpers only under windowsOnly. They are an edit-time validation rule that affects persisted geometry, so it belongs in the divergence list. Porting adds new Core code; the user decides, since 'no new features' applies. ...
- decision 2026-11-16: option (a) Port path_is_inside / path_intersects_any / attachment_fits_area and call them from the drag computation. User: "port the hole/attachment containment validation for parent-area edits"
- fix scope (set by the decision, owner rendering-planview): Port path_is_inside / path_intersects_any / attachment_fits_area (hole and attachment containment validation) and call them from the parent-area drag computation.
- outcome (fixed): Decision (a). DragController gets the validator hook DragDelegate dragShouldAcceptPosition:forTakeoff:handleIndex:lastValidPosition: (called once per step with the candidate BEFORE it is kept; a rejected step leaves the position as it was, for corner, edge and whole-shape drags alike). ViewController builds ost_hierarchy::HierarchyModel per call and calls Core ost_drag::drag... Windows evaluates polygon_is_valid on scene coordinates (OST * 72 * view_scale / scale_ratio); iOS evaluates in OST: exactly collinear/touching degenerate polygons can answer differently for scale factors that are not a power of two (no view_scale on iOS). DECIDED 2026-11-19 (keep, no code change): polygon validity is evaluated in OST coordinates. Files: `Core/Geometry/polygon_region.hpp, Core/Geometry/polygon_region.cpp, Core/Geometry/hierarchy_validation.hpp, Core/Geometry/hierarchy_validation.cpp, Controllers/ViewController/Components/DragController.h, Controllers/ViewController/Components/DragController.mm, Controllers/ViewController/ViewController.mm, Core/Geometry/drag_rules.hpp, Core/Geometry/drag_rules.cpp`.
- decision (2026-11-19): keep iOS: Polygon validity for drag candidates is evaluated in OST coordinates (Windows: scene coordinates, which depend on the render view_scale). No code change. Also covers RPV-M01 where it names the same point. Pinned by: DragCandidateValid.ParentCornerThatWouldExpelAHoleIsRejected; DragCandidateValid.AttachmentMustStayInsideItsParent; ParentContainsHoles.ParentCornerDragCannotCutThroughAHole. Test gap: The tests use OST coordinates throughout; none compares the OST result with a scene-coordinate evaluation, so the rounding-dependent difference to Windows is not asserted.

#### 109. POS-M01: Non-finite position coordinates (inf/nan) are accepted on iOS; Windows rejects them and aborts the load
- owner: parsers-units | impact: data-safety | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED (verifier-added) | status: fixed-ui-unverified
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/domain/entities/takeoff.py:84-96 (has_valid_contract uses _is_finite_number on every coordinate); ...`: parse_position('nan;2;3;4') returns [nan,2,3,4] because float() accepts it, but the Takeoff contract check then raises ValueError, so the whole bid load fails with the offending UID. Windows test: test_access_reader_rejects_duplicate_and_contract_violating_takeoffs (test_bid_data_reader.py:1171-1180) pins this for b'nan;2;3;4\n'.
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore.cpp:195 (std::stod accepts inf/nan); ProjectStore+Load.cpp:256-267`: 'inf;1' parses to [inf,1] and 'nan;1' to [nan,1] (verified with the compiled iOS parse_position). The takeoff is kept when size >= 2, so NaN or inf coordinates flow into area, length and mesh geometry.
- Windows tests: none found
- note (Phase 1): Windows aborts the whole bid load. iOS needs a decision on whether to fail the load, drop the takeoff, or sanitise. Silently keeping non-finite geometry is clearly not the Windows rule. ...
- decision 2026-11-16: option (a) Fail the bid load with an error naming the takeoff UID (Windows). User: "fail the bid open with an error naming the cause (the takeoff UID); no silent drops"
- fix scope (set by the decision, owner parsers-units): A non-finite position fails the bid open with an error naming the takeoff UID; no silent drops (same failure mechanism as DBP-31 and BRW-16).
- outcome (fixed-ui-unverified): Same call and alert as DBP-31. Files: `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Load.cpp, Services/ImportService.mm, Controllers/DatabaseViewController.mm, Controllers/ViewController/ViewController.mm`.

#### 110. IMP-M01: iOS XML parser accepts malformed files that Windows rejects and keeps raw windows-1252 bytes as invalid UTF-8
- owner: import-ost-osp | impact: data-safety | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED (verifier-added) | status: fixed
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/importers/ost_importer.py:427-433 (plain ET.parse, expat)`: Strict XML 1.0 parsing. Ran it: rejects (ParseError) a file with a raw 0xE9 byte and no encoding declaration, duplicate attributes, '<' inside an attribute value, a second root element, an undefined entity (&foo;), a bare '&', an unquoted attribute value and a U+0001 control character. ...
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Import/OstXmlParser.cpp:71-81 (pugixml load_buffer with default flags); consumer Services/ImportService.mm:344-349,360-363`: Host harness: ACCEPTS all of those malformed inputs (duplicate attribute: last wins; '<' kept; second root ignored; '&foo;' and bare '&' kept literally; 0x01 kept). For a windows-1252 declaration it keeps the raw bytes (43 61 66 E9 20 96), which is invalid UTF-8; only ISO-8859-1 is converted (C3 A9). The invalid bytes reach SQLite via bind_text. ...
- Windows tests: tests/infrastructure/mdb/importers/test_ost_importer.py::test_import_ost_rejects_unreadable_or_malformed_files_before_the_writer
- note (Phase 1): Decision needed on the approach: pugixml cannot be made strict, so reproducing expat means a pre-validation pass (libxml2/NSXMLParser, or UTF-8 validation plus a windows-1252 transcode plus duplicate-attribute checks). ...
- decision 2026-11-16: option (a) Add a pre-validation pass (libxml2/NSXMLParser, or UTF-8 validation plus windows-1252 transcode plus duplicate-attribute checks) so malformed files are rejected like Windows. User: "pre-validate XML as strictly as Windows (non-UTF-8 bytes, duplicate attributes, bad entities and so on) with a windows-1252 transcode; never produce nil strings"
- fix scope (set by the decision, owner import-ost-osp): Pre-validate the XML as strictly as Windows (non-UTF-8 bytes, duplicate attributes, bad entities and so on) with a windows-1252 transcode; malformed files are rejected; never produce nil strings.
- outcome (fixed): No pugixml load_buffer / save in ImportService any more: the OST is parsed once from its ORIGINAL bytes with parse_ost_xml (strict, windows-1252 transcode); importOSTFile shows 'Invalid OST file format: <expat reason>'; the image rewrite is applied to the parsed BidPages rows (the strict normalize_xml + pugixml tree is used read-only to find the BidPage references like ET.it... Files: `Core/Import/OstXmlParser.cpp, Core/Import/OstXmlParser.hpp, Core/Import/XmlNormalizer.hpp, Core/Import/XmlNormalizer.cpp, Core/Import/XmlCharData.hpp, Services/ImportService.mm`.

#### 111. DBP-31: load_project does no integrity validation and silently drops takeoffs; Windows refuses to load invalid data
- owner: database-persistence | impact: data-safety | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed-ui-unverified
- aliases: BRW-20, LP-M04
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/bid_data_reader.py:144-188,1112-1115,1195-1200; infrastructure/database/bid_owned_identity.py:105-124,238-294; ...`: Raises on malformed or duplicate UIDs (positive integers only), a takeoff referencing a missing condition, page or parent, a hole on a different page than its parent, ParentUID cycles, non-finite rotation/position, and folder cycles. Takeoffs with an empty position are kept (position []).
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Load.cpp:238-270, 296-344`: No checks. Takeoffs with a missing condition or fewer than 2 coordinates are dropped silently (host load: 5 rows inserted, 4 loaded); missing pages, parents, cycles and bad UIDs are tolerated; folder cycles silently remove the folders.
- Windows tests: tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_bid_data_reader.py:4204,4250 (test_a_non_finite_rotation_violates_the_takeoff_contract_with_its_uid); tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_hierarchy_reader.py:218,235,311,380,391; tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_bid_data_reader.py::test_bid_load_rejects_takeoff_with_missing_required_owner (+4 more, see PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json)
- note (Phase 1): A strict load that aborts a whole project on one bad row is a product decision on a phone.
- decision 2026-11-16: option (a) Fail the open with an error alert (Windows). User: "fail the bid open with an error naming the cause (the missing reference); no silent drops"
- fix scope (set by the decision, owner database-persistence): An integrity problem fails the bid open with an error naming the cause (the missing reference); no silent drops of takeoffs.
- outcome (fixed-ui-unverified): The post-duplicate reload uses loadProjectWithUID:project:error:; a failure keeps the project and shows 'Error Loading File' / 'Failed to load <bid>: <cause>'. Files: `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Load.cpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore_Integrity.hpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Integrity.cpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore.hpp, Services/ImportService.h, Services/ImportService.mm, Controllers/DatabaseViewController.mm, Controllers/ViewController/ViewController.mm`.

## (C) Behavior

90 items (including the re-baseline items RB-01..RB-04), global rank order. All former `needs-decision` items are decided (section E); `cannot-reproduce` items are in section F; `wont-fix` and `no-longer-divergent` items stay listed with their reason. Each block ends with its final `outcome` (status, what was done, files); the `Windows:` and `iOS (as mapped):` lines describe the two sides when the map was made, so the iOS side is the code before the fix.

#### 112. LP-16: PlanView ignores page_index, rotation, flip and the real PDF page size
- owner: rendering-planview | impact: behavior | disposition: fix | testable: objc-only | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed-ui-unverified
- aliases: RPV-18, RPV-19

- iOS (as mapped): `PlanView/Components/PDFCanvasView.mm:18-19,43; PlanView/PlanView.mm:126-188`: Always draws CGPDFDocumentGetPage(doc, 1) with rotation 0, never flips the background (takeoffs get flipped by ost_to_pdf_coordinates), and uses the stored Width/Height*72 as the canvas, aspect-fitting the PDF to it.

Ships together with GEO-06, RPV-02, RPV-03; also RPV-M04 (count symbol orientation) follows from a view-level page transform. Windows uses the crop/visible box size; ...
- outcome (fixed-ui-unverified): The PDF page BidPages.Index1 shows is drawn (ost_page_view::normalized_pdf_page_index: negative = 0, beyond the end = last page), the page size is the PDF's own (crop box, /Rotate applied: ost_page_view::effective_pdf_page_size from CGPDFPageGetBoxRect(kCGPDFCropBox) + CGPDFPageGetRotationAngle), else BidPages Width/Height * 72, else 612 x 792 (resolve_pdf_page_size); the ca... [UI] PDF page choice and sizes are Core-tested; the PDF drawing under rotation, flips, multi-page and crop box (PDFCanvasView) needs a device check. Files: `PlanView/PlanView.mm, PlanView/PlanView.h, PlanView/PlanView_Internal.h, PlanView/Components/PDFCanvasView.h, PlanView/Components/PDFCanvasView.mm, Core/Geometry/page_view.hpp, Core/Geometry/page_view.cpp, Controllers/ViewController/ViewController.mm`.

#### 113. LP-08: Pages are ordered by Index1/Name instead of BidPages.Sequence/Name/UID; folder pages and subfolders are laid out differently
- owner: layers-pages | impact: behavior | disposition: fix | testable: needs-extraction-from-objc | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- aliases: BRW-18, DBP-27
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/bid_data_reader.py:711-713; domain/entities/bid.py:80-99; presentation/components/page_combo.py:174-205,230-245,486-487`: Pages are ordered by Sequence, then Name, then UID. Folders are ordered by Name then UID. Within a folder, subfolders come first, then the folder's own pages; loose pages come last. get_first_page_uid is the first page in that tree walk. Index1 is only the PDF page index.
- iOS (as mapped): `Services/ProjectService.mm:49-54,60-63; Controllers/ViewController/Components/ViewController+PageSelector.mm:104-190`: BidPages.Sequence and SheetNo are not loaded. pageList is sorted by (Index1-1, name) using byte order, folderOrder by name only with std::sort (unstable), and a folder's own pages are shown before its subfolders. With several PDFs (each Index1 starting at 1) pages interleave. The default first page is lowest Index1.
- Windows tests: tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_hierarchy_reader.py::test_page_info_carries_every_stored_field_in_sequence_name_uid_order; ::test_pages_with_equal_sequence_and_name_are_ordered_by_uid; ::test_folders_nest_by_parent_drop_empty_branches_and_keep_name_order (+5 more, see PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json)
- note (Phase 1): Access ORDER BY on Name is locale and case-insensitive while SQLite BINARY (the tests) is case-sensitive; Windows prod behavior for case ties is therefore implicit. Needs Sequence (and SheetNo) loaded in ProjectStore+Load.cpp; ...
- outcome (fixed): Pages are listed in the Windows order (BidPages Sequence, Name, UID) and laid out like the Windows page tree (folders by Name/UID, a folder's subfolders first, then its own pages, loose pages last). Core: ProjectData::page_order (the SQL order, set by load_project) and ost_data::page_tree_order (Core/Data/PageNavigation.*); ProjectService.loadProject builds pageList from it ... Files: `Core/Data/ProjectData.hpp, Core/Data/PageNavigation.hpp, Core/Data/PageNavigation.cpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Load.cpp, Services/ProjectService.mm, Controllers/ViewController/Components/ViewController+PageSelector.mm`.

#### 120. EXP-13: OBJ face winding is inside-out for cylinders (circle/ellipse count) and curved walls
- owner: meshing | impact: behavior | disposition: wont-fix (Phase 1: fix; was needs-decision until 2026-11-19) | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: wont-fix
- aliases: MSH-M02
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/core/boolean_operations.py:49-54,58-61 (unflipped without negatives, flipped with); ...`: Every box-like, cylinder and curved mesh comes out outward-facing (positive signed volume) after the vertex rotation and face swap. Verified: circle d=24 h=48 gives +21467.487; curved wall (0,200)-(100,200) via (50,230), curve=0, h=96, t=6 gives +70478.256; straight/sloped wall, rotated box, area flat and sloped, and attachment all positive. ...
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Meshing/gen_count.cpp (cylinder), Core/Meshing/gen_curved.cpp; Core/Export/obj_writer.cpp:138-143 (faces written as stored)`: Box meshes are outward-facing, but cylinder meshes (circle, and ellipse routed to the cylinder) and curved walls are inside-out. Harness signed volumes: circle -21467.487, curved wall -70478.256, ellipse -21467.487 (the ellipse geometry is also wrong; see the mesh area).
- Windows tests: tests/presentation/visualization/exporters/adapters/test_obj_writer.py::OBJMeshWriterTests::test_coordinate_winding_and_multi_mesh_indices_without_mutating_input (pre-existing, unchanged)
- note (Phase 1, superseded where it conflicts with the re-baseline): The root cause is in the meshing area. It is visible in OBJ viewers with backface culling, where the cylinders look empty or black. Windows itself writes the isosceles triangle prism inside-out (signed volume -6912 for w=24,d=12,h=48), so do not copy that quirk when triangle prisms are ported. ...
- rebaselined 2026-11-16 (disposition stays fix): Plan caveat only: the Windows OBJ face winding of an untouched positive depends on whether the export contains a negative mesh (apply_boolean_operations flips every positive when one exists, and the OBJ writer flips again), so the 'positive signed volume' oracle holds only for exports without negative takeoffs (the existing plan already says so).
- Windows now: Unchanged without negatives: wall [0,0,100,0] height 96 thickness 6 -> OBJ signed volume +57600 (verified again, 8 vertices, 12 faces); slab 100x100x6 +60000. With any negative mesh in the export the untouched positives come out with the opposite sign (-57600, -60000).
- iOS now: Unchanged (cylinders and curved walls inside-out on iOS).
- outcome (wont-fix): Cylinders and curved walls are outward-facing (positive volume) and wired. Every exported mesh stays outward-facing, with or without a negative. DECIDED 2026-11-19 (keep iOS, no code change; alias MSH-M02 follows; same line as EXP-M02 / GEO-15 / EXP-17). Windows core/boolean_operations.py:49-61 + adapters/obj_writer.py:38-43 flips every positive when any negative exists and swaps faces again, so one OBJ mixes both windings: possible Windows defect (inconsistent). Pinned by ObjWinding.BooleanResultsStayOutwardFacingWithANegativeInTheExport. Files: `Core/Meshing/gen_count.cpp, Core/Meshing/gen_curved.cpp`.
- decision (2026-11-19): keep iOS: Every exported OBJ mesh stays outward-facing, with or without a negative of another type in the export. No code change. Covers the alias MSH-M02 (same line as the decided EXP-M02 / GEO-15 / EXP-17). Pinned by: ObjWinding.BooleanResultsStayOutwardFacingWithANegativeInTheExport; ObjWinding.ExportedMeshesHaveThePositiveSignedVolumeOfTheWindowsObj; ObjWinding.EveryCurvedWallAndCylinderVariantIsOutwardFacing.
- possible Windows defect: ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/core/boolean_operations.py:10-12; ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/core/boolean_operations.py:49-61; cpp_extensions/src/module_geometry.cpp:58-71; cpp_extensions/src/module_geometry.cpp:95-103; cpp_extensions/src/boolean/boolean_ops.cpp:225-229; cpp_extensions/src/boolean/boolean_ops.cpp:781-788; ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/adapters/obj_writer.py:38-43; tests/presentation/visualization/core/test_boolean_operations.py:11; tests/presentation/visualization/utils/test_mesh.py:234-241: boolean_operations.py flips every mesh the native call returns, but the native code returns meshes with two different windings: meshes that went through the boolean come out normalised (outward) and the Python flip turns them into the generators' inward convention (the OBJ writer's face swap then makes them outward in the file), whereas positives that have no same-type negative in the export are returned untouched (already inward) and the same flip turns them outward (the OBJ swap then makes them inward). So one OBJ mixes both windings, and adding a negative anywhere in the export turns every untouched positive of another type inside-out. Scenario: A bid with a wall condition and, on another condition type, a negative area: the exported OBJ shows the wall's faces inward and the cut slab outward. Viewers that cull back faces (and slicers that read the signed volume) show or treat the wall as inside-out; removing the negative flips the wall back. Severity: inconsistent, confidence: medium. Suggested Windows fix: Flip only meshes that Manifold produced: the native apply_boolean_operations already marks them (metadata boolean_applied at module_geometry.cpp:90); make boolean_operations.py:59 flip only entries with that flag, or normalise winding once (for example by signed volume) after the pass. Add a signed-volume test with a cross-type negative.

#### 122. EXP-12: Name sanitisation rules differ (OBJ group/material and DXF layer)
- owner: export-formats | impact: behavior | disposition: wont-fix (Phase 1: fix; was needs-decision until 2026-11-19) | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: wont-fix
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/base_exporter.py:175-201 (_sanitize_material_name; was 172-198)`: Replaces ' ' '/' '\\' ':' ';' '*' '?' '<' '>' '|' '.' '(' ')' '[' ']' '{' '}' with '_', and removes '"' "'" ','. All other characters are kept (non-ASCII, #, &, =, %, +, -, @). A leading digit gets the 'Mat_' prefix. An empty result becomes 'Unknown'.
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Export/obj_writer.cpp:84-102`: Whitelist: keeps ASCII alnum, '_' and '-'; ' ' becomes '_'; every other byte is dropped (all UTF-8 non-ASCII bytes vanish); no digit prefix; empty becomes 'mesh'. DXF layer names are not sanitised at all (ExportService.mm:144).
- Windows tests: tests/presentation/visualization/exporters/test_obj_exporter.py
- note (Phase 1, superseded where it conflicts with the re-baseline): Apply the Windows sanitiser to DXF layer names too. DXF forbids <>/\":;?*|=` in layer names.
- rebaselined 2026-11-16 (disposition stays fix): base_exporter.py line shift (+3 after line 163).
- outcome (wont-fix): Sanitisation rule and DXF layer name = OBJ material name are wired and tested. Control characters are dropped from exported OBJ group / material / DXF layer names. DECIDED 2026-11-19 (keep iOS, no code change). Windows base_exporter.py:175-201 keeps ASCII control characters (a line break in a condition name injects OBJ/MTL/DXF statements): possible Windows defect (unsafe). Pinned by ExportText.SanitizeMaterialNameDropsControlCharactersUnlikeWindows. Files: `Core/Export/export_text.cpp, Core/Export/export_text.hpp, Core/Export/UnicodeDigitData.hpp, Core/Export/obj_writer.cpp, Core/Export/obj_writer.hpp, Services/ExportService.mm`.
- decision (2026-11-19): keep iOS: Control characters (U+0000-U+001F, U+007F) are dropped from exported OBJ group / material names and DXF layer names; Windows keeps them. No code change. Pinned by: ExportText.SanitizeMaterialNameDropsControlCharactersUnlikeWindows; ObjWriter.GroupAndMaterialNamesAreSanitisedLikeWindows.
- possible Windows defect: ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/base_exporter.py:175-201; ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/base_exporter.py:135-147; ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/obj_exporter.py:45; ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/obj_exporter.py:71-72; ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/dxf_exporter.py:21-22; ost_visualizer/presentation/dialogs/edit_condition_dialog.py:1410: The sanitiser keeps CR, LF, TAB, NUL and other control characters. OBJ, MTL and DXF are line based, so a condition name with a line break corrupts or injects statements into the exported files (and makes the DXF layer table structurally invalid). No Windows test covers a control character. Scenario: Condition named 'Wall' + line break + 'usemtl Evil' (pasted, or imported from an OST file): the OBJ/MTL/DXF export reports success but the files contain broken or injected statements and the DXF is invalid for CAD readers. Severity: unsafe, confidence: high. Suggested Windows fix: In _sanitize_material_name (base_exporter.py:176-201) also replace every character with ord < 0x20, U+007F and the Unicode line separators U+0085, U+2028, U+2029 by '_' (for example re.sub(r'[\x00-\x1f\x7f\u0085\u2028\u2029]', '_', sanitized)). Add a test with a name containing LF and CR.

No code change. Scenario: Condition name 'A' + LF + 'newmtl Evil' + LF + 'Kd 0 0 1': the exported MTL defines an extra material 'Evil' with a colour the user never set, and the OBJ can be made to select it. Severity: unsafe, confidence: high. Suggested Windows fix: Write the comment from a single-line copy of the name (replace CR, LF and other control characters by a space) at obj_exporter.py:46 and :70, together with the EXP-12 fix.

- possible Windows defect: ost_visualizer/application/use_cases/project/condition_summary_service.py:436; ost_visualizer/application/use_cases/project/condition_summary_service.py:487-491; ost_visualizer/application/services/summary_csv_export_service.py:208; ost_visualizer/presentation/components/conditions_sidebar.py:658-666: Because the loader always fills cdn_type_name with 'Unknown', the 'or SUMMARY_UNASSIGNED_LABEL' fallback at condition_summary_service.py:436 never fires for a loaded condition. The same type-less condition is labelled 'Unknown' in the summary row / CSV type column but '(unassigned)' as a type group and in the sidebar tree. Scenario: Condition without a type: the summary row and the CSV 'Type' column say 'Unknown' while the type group header and the conditions sidebar say '(unassigned)'. Severity: cosmetic, confidence: medium. Suggested Windows fix: In _condition_values use the same rule as _group_label: cdn_type_name if cdn_type_uid else SUMMARY_UNASSIGNED_LABEL.

#### 132. COND-PLACE-01: Conditions on a hidden layer can still be edited and used for placing on iOS
- owner: conditions | impact: behavior | disposition: fix | testable: objc-only | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed-ui-unverified
- aliases: LP-17, RPV-21
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/placement_coordinator.py:52-72,127-129; presentation/components/conditions_sidebar.py:310-335,712-725,858-870`: A condition whose layer is hidden (layer_visible False) is not placeable (Placement.enter returns False and exits placement), its sidebar row is greyed with no inline rename, and the Edit button / double-click edit are disabled for it.
- iOS (as mapped): `Controllers/ViewController/Components/ViewController+Conditions.mm:45-65; Controllers/ConditionsViewController.mm:389-405`: didSelectConditionUID always enters PlaceTakeoff mode (no layer-visibility check), the row is not greyed, and the detail button opens the edit dialog for any condition.
- Windows tests: tests/presentation/coordinators/test_placement_coordinator.py::test_enter_rejects_unplaceable_requests_without_activating_plan_view; tests/presentation/components/test_conditions_sidebar.py::test_double_click_edit_dialog_follows_properties_permission_and_placeability; tests/integration/visibility/test_deferred_layers.py (+2 more, see PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json)
- note (Phase 1): Windows also suspends an active placement tool when its layer is hidden and restores it on Show.
- outcome (fixed-ui-unverified): Both open halves done. Core (host-tested first, 9 CondPlace01_LayerHide / CondPlace01_LayerShow tests, written before the code and failing to build without it): suspended_placement_for_layer_hide (Windows _active_layer_tool_snapshot place branch: the layer of the placed condition or Hide All suspends it, a layerless condition never) and restore_of_suspended_placement (_restore_suspended_layer_tool: other layer keeps, another tool / revision / bid / vanished, retyped, moved or still hidden condition / not the 2D view drops, else restores). Obj-C: ViewController+Layers.mm single toggle and Show All / Hide All call _suspendPlacementForLayerHide (before the layers change, via _leavePlacement, remembering the tool revision) and _restorePlacementAfterLayerShow (last, via _selectConditionForPlacement:Enter) and reload the Conditions list ([condVC loadConditionsFromProject:] + reselect) so the greyed rows follow. ViewController.mm: ivar _suspendedPlacement + tool revision counter bumped in _setEditMode: when the mode changes (Windows PlanView.tool_revision); ViewController_Internal.h declares the accessors and the two Conditions helpers. Adaptations: Windows 'cursor mode is select' = iOS View mode (what _leavePlacement returns to); the Windows condition-object identity check is a style + layer comparison (iOS conditions are values); no annotation tool, one placed condition, no permission check on iOS. Status check (final runs of 2026-11-19): host tests 1598 pass (MSVC), Conditions 174 pass on the clang+fma build, Obj-C syntax check 27/27 files 0 errors (4 baseline warnings). [UI] The Layers sheet / FAB / Conditions list behavior is only syntax-checked against hand-written stubs; needs a device check: place with a condition, hide its layer (mode goes to View, FAB place button off, row greyed), show it again (placement resumes), and hide, pick another tool, show (nothing resumes). The list reload only matters while the Conditions sheet is alive (conditionsVC is weak; reopening it always loads fresh). Files: `Core/Domain/ConditionChoices.hpp, Config/Tests/Conditions/test_condition_choices.cpp, Controllers/ViewController/Components/ViewController+Layers.mm, Controllers/ViewController/ViewController.mm, Controllers/ViewController/ViewController_Internal.h (earlier: Controllers/ViewController/Components/ViewController+Conditions.mm, Controllers/ConditionsViewController.mm)`.
- possible Windows defect (note 2: layer-less conditions on Hide All): ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/ui_event_coordinator.py:1473-1489; ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/ui_event_coordinator.py:6721-6724; ost_visualizer/domain/services/project_data_service.py:664-678; ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/placement_coordinator.py:127-129; ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/components/placement_mode.py:1647-1650; tests/presentation/coordinators/test_ui_event_coordinator.py:18860-18864: Consequence of the LP-22 root cause: while placing a layer-less condition, Hide All does not suspend the tool (the snapshot requires a layer, pinned by a Windows test) but marks that condition hidden in memory, so the placement tool stays armed on a condition that is unplaceable until a later reconcile; Show All restores nothing because nothing was suspended. The refusal of the snapshot is deliberate and test-pinned, so the defect is the in-memory hide of layer-less conditions (LP-22), not the snapshot rule. Scenario: Place with a layer-less condition, click Hide All: the placement cursor stays active but taps do nothing until another action reconciles the placement. Severity: inconsistent, confidence: low. Suggested Windows fix: Same as LP-22: make update_all_layer_visibility skip layer-less conditions.
- open question (still open, not decided): note 1: Image-layer exemption on hide. Hiding the Image layer. WINDOWS ui_event_coordinator.py:6473-6476 skips _suspend_active_layer_tool when the hidden layer is the Image layer (and :6503-6504 skips the restore), so a placement of a condition on the Image layer keeps running on a condition that is hidden and unplaceable (a tap is ignored, placement_mode.py:1648). IOS: _suspendPlacementForLayerHide treats the Image layer like any other layer and leaves the placement tool. ASK: copy the exemption? (judged an edge case, not a Windows defect; low confidence) Windows: ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/ui_event_coordinator.py:6473-6476; ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/ui_event_coordinator.py:6503-6504; ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/placement_coordinator.py:127-129; ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/placement_coordinator.py:163-189; ost_visualizer/domain/services/project_data_service.py:651-653; ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/components/placement_mode.py:1648.
- open question (still open, not decided): note 2: layer-less conditions on Hide All. Hide All with an armed placement of a layer-less condition. WINDOWS ui_event_coordinator.py:1473-1489 never suspends the tool for a layer-less condition (pinned by a test) but project_data_service.py:664-678 marks every condition hidden on Hide All, so the tool stays armed on an unplaceable condition (possible Windows defect, consequence of LP-22). IOS: a layer-less condition is never suspended either (suspended_placement_for_layer_hide); Hide All writes only layer visibility and a layer-less condition stays visible and placeable (LP-22 stays wont-fix). ASK: nothing to port unless the Windows behavior is wanted byte for byte. Windows: ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/ui_event_coordinator.py:1473-1489; ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/ui_event_coordinator.py:6721-6724; ost_visualizer/domain/services/project_data_service.py:664-678; ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/placement_coordinator.py:127-129; ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/components/placement_mode.py:1647-1650; tests/presentation/coordinators/test_ui_event_coordinator.py:18860-18864.

#### 137. BRW-09: Restore from Deleted Bids ignores OrigBidProjectUID; iOS also allows moving trash bids to any folder
- owner: project-browser | impact: behavior | disposition: wont-fix (Phase 1: fix; was needs-decision until 2026-11-19) | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: wont-fix

- iOS (as mapped): `Controllers/DatabaseViewController.mm:371-457; ProjectStore+Folders.cpp:191-206`: Trash bids get 'Restore as Unassigned' (BidProjectUID=NULL; OrigBidProjectUID is never consulted), and also the 'Move to Folder' menu, which re-homes a trash bid into any folder.

- outcome (wont-fix): Restore from Deleted Bids is done (ost_store::restore_bid, Restore menu entry, no Move/Remove for trash bids). A dangling OrigBidProjectUID (folder gone) restores the bid unassigned. DECIDED 2026-11-19 (keep iOS, no code change; alias DBP-22 follows). Windows project_write_handler.py:580-610 + project_operations.py:38-45 fails the whole restore group: judged a deliberate referential-integrity rule, not a Windows defect (the dangling state is unreachable inside Windows). Pinned by Brw09_Restore.AfterTheOriginalFolderWasDeletedTheBidComesBackUnassigned. Files: `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore.hpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore.cpp, Services/ImportService.h, Services/ImportService.mm, Controllers/DatabaseViewController.mm`.
- decision (2026-11-19): keep iOS: Restoring a bid from Deleted Bids whose OrigBidProjectUID folder no longer exists restores it unassigned (Windows refuses the whole restore group). No code change. Covers the alias DBP-22. Pinned by: Brw09_Restore.AfterTheOriginalFolderWasDeletedTheBidComesBackUnassigned; Brw09_Restore.InterimADanglingOriginalFolderRestoresTheBidUnassigned.

#### 146. RPV-20: Polygon close detection uses the snapped tap point and a strict canvas distance; Windows uses the raw cursor and <= 12 viewport px
- owner: rendering-planview | impact: behavior | disposition: fix | testable: needs-extraction-from-objc | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- Windows: `placement_mode.py:1697-1730`: If len(points) >= 3 and the raw cursor viewport position is within 12 px (<=) of the first point, the polygon closes (if valid) without appending a point.
- iOS (as mapped): `PlanView+Placement.mm:297-322`: Computes the tap OST after grid rounding and angle snapping from the last point, converts that back to canvas and tests distance < 12/zoom, so angle snapping can pull the point more than 12 pt away from the first point and prevent closing.
- Windows tests: tests/presentation/components/plan_view/components/test_placement_mode.py (area click placement tests)
- note (Phase 1): Closing an invalid polygon neither flashes nor appends, on both sides.
- outcome (fixed): Core ost_placement::polygon_close_hit (>= 3 points and raw cursor <= 12 px, inclusive). PlanView _handlePlaceTap measures the RAW tap against the first point in screen points (canvas distance * zoomScale) before any snapping; an invalid polygon neither closes nor appends. The strict canvas-distance test on the snapped point is removed. Files: `Core/Geometry/placement_snap.hpp, Core/Geometry/placement_snap.cpp, PlanView/Components/PlanView+Placement.mm`.

DECIDED 2026-11-19 (keep, no code change): the constant is accepted (minimum interactive sizes 8/3 pt and 2/3 pt). Files: `PlanView/PlanView.mm, PlanView/PlanView.h, PlanView/PlanView_Internal.h, PlanView/Components/PlanView+Placement.mm, Core/Geometry/takeoff_footprint.hpp, Core/Geometry/takeoff_footprint.cpp`.
- decision (2026-11-19): keep iOS: The constant ost_footprint::kInteractivePdfRenderScale (3.0) is accepted: the minimum interactive sizes are 8/3 pt (count item) and 2/3 pt (linear band) on the iOS canvas. No code change. Pinned by: FootprintSizeRules.MinimumPointTakeoffSizeScalesBothSides; FootprintSizeRules.PortOfLinearThicknessScalesWithViewScaleAndHasAMinimum; FootprintOnCanvas.LinearMinimumThicknessCanBeRaised. Test gap: The value 3.0 itself is not asserted by a test (the tests pass an explicit view_scale or the minimum).

- note (Phase 1): Requires a new UndoAction kind for property edits.
- outcome (fixed-ui-unverified): The 'Count as Negative Quantity' toggle (ViewController _setNegative:forPressedTakeoff:) is ONE history entry (UndoAction::SetNegative holding {uid, old, new}, UndoRedoController pushNegativeChanges:) and is undoable/redoable. DECIDED 2026-11-19 (code change, port): it changes ONLY the pressed takeoff (ost_domain::negative_changes_for_press in Core/Domain/NegativeChange.hpp), like Windows on_set_negative for a single selected takeoff (plan_view_action_handler.py:2072-2116). A condition that is merely selected in the sidebar widens nothing any more (before: every takeoff of the condition on the open pages). Tests: Und09_NegativePress.* in test_negative_press.cpp. Unverified: the context menu / undo / redo path was only syntax-checked against stubs, not run on a device. Files: `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore.hpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Takeoffs.cpp, Services/ImportService.h, Services/ImportService.mm, Controllers/ViewController/Components/UndoRedoController.h, Controllers/ViewController/Components/UndoRedoController.mm, Controllers/ViewController/ViewController.mm, Core/Domain/NegativeChange.hpp, Config/Tests/Settings/test_negative_press.cpp`.
- decision (2026-11-19): code change, port the Windows behavior: 'Count as Negative Quantity' changes ONLY the pressed takeoff, in ONE undoable entry (like Windows on_set_negative for a single selected takeoff). A condition that is merely selected in the sidebar no longer widens the change to every takeoff of the condition on the open pages. Pinned by: Und09_NegativePress.AConditionSelectedInTheSidebarWidensNothing; Und09_NegativePress.TheOtherTakeoffsOfTheConditionStayOutOfTheEntry; Und09_NegativePress.TheOldFlagOfThePressedTakeoffIsRecorded; Und09_NegativePress.AnUnknownUidGivesNoChange; Und09_NegativePress.AnEntryWritesTheOldFlagOnUndoAndTheNewFlagOnRedo; Und09_NegativePress.OneUndoRestoresThePressedTakeoffAndOneRedoAppliesItAgain.
- code change (2026-11-19): files: Core/Domain/NegativeChange.hpp (new); Controllers/ViewController/ViewController.mm; Controllers/ViewController/Components/UndoRedoController.h; Controllers/ViewController/Components/UndoRedoController.mm; Config/Tests/Settings/test_negative_press.cpp (new). Tests added: Und09_NegativePress.* (6 tests). Failing first: the Core press decision was first added with the old rule (a condition selected in the sidebar widens the change to every takeoff of the condition on the open pages); against it 5 of the 6 new tests failed on assertions (for example changes.size 3 != 1, and in the undo chain test takeoff 500 was changed too) and 1 passed (AnUnknownUidGivesNoChange, which the old rule already met): TESTS: 6 PASSED: 1 FAILED: 5. The red run was against that old rule placed in Core, not against the .mm (the host harness cannot run .mm); negative_values was moved verbatim from the .mm. Review: CONFIRMED-FIXED by an independent reviewer, who ran three mutations of NegativeChange.hpp (the file was restored byte-identically, checked by sha256): widening the press to the whole condition (the old behavior) failed 5 of 6 tests; recording no old flag and returning a change for an unknown uid failed 5 of 6; swapping use_old in negative_values failed 2 of 6 (undo and redo swapped). The .mm call site cannot be mutated on the host, so the reviewer read it. The Core test has no selection input: it proves one change per press with the old flag recorded, not that the .mm passes the right uid and flag. The reviewer also diffed the three .mm/.h files against the version before the change (the selection logic moved into ost_domain::negative_changes_for_press, one history entry, undo and redo through negative_values). The tests exercise Core only; _setNegative:forPressedTakeoff:, the context menu and the undo buttons are only syntax-checked against stubs. Not verified: The context-menu / undo / redo path was not run on a device (fixed-ui-unverified).

Distance = 1.80278*size, elevation 56.31 deg, fixed azimuth on the +y (page-bottom) side, fov 45 deg. The bounds also include the 3D page plane (plan texture).
- iOS (as mapped): `Rendering/Renderer.mm:455-481,536-551; Controllers/ViewController/Components/CameraController.mm:31-39`: Y-up. target = bbox centre. distance = max(2.5*maxExtent, 3.0). yaw 0.5 rad, pitch 0.45 rad (about 25.8 deg), eye = target + dist*(cos p sin yaw, sin p, cos p cos yaw). Because iOS maps z = -ost_y (MeshFactory.cpp:26), +z is the page-top side, while Windows looks from the +y page-bottom side. Orientation is derived from code, so confirm on device.
- Windows tests: tests/integration/native/test_renderer_boundaries.py:30-67; tests/presentation/components/test_mesh_view.py:1638
- note (Phase 1): Values taken from running the Windows native Camera. The near and far planes also differ (iOS: near=max(dist*0.005,0.01), far=max(dist+2R,4R), since 2026-11-20 also at least the farthest corner of the live scene bounds (Core/Domain/SceneRendering.hpp); Windows: near=max(0.1,0.001*size), far=max(10000,20*size)), which is platform-adapted.
- outcome (fixed): New Core header Core/Domain/CameraFraming.hpp: frame_camera(CameraPose, optional<SceneBounds>) = Windows Camera::show_object in renderer space: size = largest bounds side (100 when < 1), target = bounds centre, distance size*sqrt(1+1.5^2) = 1.80278*size, pitch atan(1.5) (56.31 deg), yaw pi (looks from the page-bottom side: Windows offset (0, size, 1.5 size) mapped (X,Y,Z)->(... Files: `Core/Domain/CameraFraming.hpp, Rendering/Renderer.mm, Controllers/ViewController/Components/CameraController.mm`.

#### 159. DLG-FINITE-01: Non-finite / overflowing numeric text is accepted on iOS
- owner: parsers-units | impact: behavior | disposition: fix | testable: needs-extraction-from-objc | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- aliases: COND-DIM-02
- Windows: `dimension_format_service.py:92-98,100-165 (_finite_float -> None for nan/inf/overflow); edit_condition_dialog.py:1389-1403,1413-1424 (isfinite checks) and 1509,1544,1556 ...`: 'nan', 'inf', '-inf', 'Infinity', 1e400 and a 5000-digit run all return None / alert 'is not a valid dimension for <label>'.
- iOS (as mapped): `Controllers/EditConditionViewController/Components/EditConditionHelpers.mm:208-306 (intValue feet saturates at INT_MAX; NSScanner fallback at :299-304); ...`: A 5000-digit feet value becomes 2147483647*12 inches and is accepted; per Apple's NSScanner documentation scanDouble returns HUGE_VAL on overflow, so '1e400' is accepted as +inf; whether the text 'inf'/'nan' is accepted by scanDouble is unverified without a device. No isfinite check anywhere in the dialog.
- Windows tests: tests/domain/services/test_dimension_format_service.py::DimensionFormattingTests::test_dimension_text_rejects_non_finite_values_before_formatting; ::DimensionParsingTableTests::test_overflowing_digit_runs_are_rejected_in_every_layout; ::test_unparseable_text_and_unsupported_fractions_are_rejected (+4 more, see PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json)
- note (Phase 1): Whether NSScanner accepts inf/nan is unverified; the overflow cases are certain per documentation. NSScanner acceptance of inf/nan could not be verified without a device; use strtod with full consumption plus isfinite.
- outcome (fixed): NSRegularExpression / NSScanner parsing deleted; the focus-out normalisation calls ost_domain::display_to_inches directly; validation goes through parse_dimension_value / parse_elevation_z / parse_slope_value / parse_display_size. Windows float() accepts digit separators and Unicode digits (ported as Windows). DECIDED 2026-11-19 (port, ONLY the Rise/Run half): a whitespace-only Rise/Run is now rejected with "Rise must be a number." / "Run must be a number." like Windows (parse_slope_value takes only an EMPTY text as 0; Windows edit_condition_dialog.py:1539, :1551, :1569, :1581 `float(text or "0")`); tests DlgFinite01_RiseRun.* (the new test failed first); the .mm hands the raw text to Core (comment change only). STILL OPEN (not decided): the condition number above the int range: iOS refuses 2147483648 and up (ConditionFormRules.cpp parse_condition_number, INT_MAX), Windows edit_condition_dialog.py:1449-1469 accepts any whole number >= 1. Files: `Core/Domain/DimensionFormat.hpp, Core/Domain/DimensionFormat.cpp, Core/Domain/PositionParser.hpp, Core/Domain/PositionParser.cpp, Core/Domain/ConditionFormRules.cpp, Core/Domain/ConditionFormRules.hpp, Config/Tests/Conditions/test_condition_form.cpp`.
- decision (2026-11-19): code change, port the Windows behavior: Whitespace-only Rise and Run are rejected with 'Rise must be a number.' / 'Run must be a number.' like Windows (only an EMPTY field is 0). ONLY this half is decided: the condition-number half (iOS refuses 2147483648 and up, Windows accepts up to the 64-bit column) is NOT decided and stays open. Pinned by: DlgFinite01_RiseRun.AWhiteSpaceOnlyFieldIsNotANumberLikeWindows; DlgFinite01_RiseRun.WhiteSpaceAroundANumberAndTheOtherRefusalsStayAsTheyWere; CondVal01_RiseRun.AnEmptyFieldIsZeroAndANumberIsTheNumber; CondVal01_RiseRun.OnlyFloatWhiteSpaceSurroundsANumber; CondVal01_RiseRun.NonFiniteAndNonNumericTextHaveTheirWindowsMessages.
- code change (2026-11-19): files: Core/Domain/ConditionFormRules.cpp; Core/Domain/ConditionFormRules.hpp (comment); Controllers/EditConditionViewController/EditConditionViewController.mm (comment only); Config/Tests/Conditions/test_condition_form.cpp. Tests added: DlgFinite01_RiseRun.AWhiteSpaceOnlyFieldIsNotANumberLikeWindows; DlgFinite01_RiseRun.WhiteSpaceAroundANumberAndTheOtherRefusalsStayAsTheyWere. Tests changed: CondVal01_RiseRun.ABlankFieldIsZeroAndANumberIsTheNumber -> renamed AnEmptyFieldIsZeroAndANumberIsTheNumber (the "   " case that pinned the old behavior is replaced by the empty-field case). Failing first: The new whitespace test failed on 15 texts x Rise/Run x Linear/Area before the change (CHECK(issue.has_value()) failed; value 0 != -1); a filtered run gave 5 tests, 4 passed and 1 failed (DlgFinite01_RiseRun.AWhiteSpaceOnlyFieldIsNotANumberLikeWindows). Review: CONFIRMED-FIXED by an independent reviewer. Mutation: putting back the old strip in parse_slope_value makes the new whitespace test fail on 120 assertions (60 rows) while the empty-field, C0-separator and neighbouring-behavior tests keep passing; the file was restored byte-identically (sha256). Other plausible wrong fixes are caught as well (rejecting empty text, the finite-number wording, stripping around a number). Limit: that UITextField.text returns exactly what was typed or pasted is an assumption; Rise and Run use a decimal pad with no space key, so a whitespace-only value arrives only by paste or an external keyboard. Checked as well: the diff against the version before the change (the Core change in parse_slope_value plus comments); the .mm hands the raw field text to Core (Rise and Run are not in the focus-out normalisation set OSTIsDimensionTag).
- open question (still open, not decided): the condition number above the int range. Windows `ost_visualizer/presentation/dialogs/edit_condition_dialog.py:1449-1469` accepts any whole number >= 1 (up to the 64-bit column), iOS refuses 2147483648 and up; see Still open in section E.

The selection border of a condition reuses the same footprint (the duplicated shape ... - Windows tests: tests/domain/services/test_project_data_service.py::test_show_all_layer_visibility_updates_conditions_and_pages_immediately; tests/domain/entities/test_layer.py; tests/domain/services/test_project_data_service.py
- note (Phase 1): Windows is self-inconsistent for layer-less conditions (session-only hide vs always visible on load); real OST conditions normally have a layer so impact is low. Verifier: Windows update_all_layer_visibility sets Condition.layer_visible for EVERY condition (including ones with no or an unknown layer ...
- decision 2026-11-16: option (b) Keep the load rule (layer-less conditions always visible). User: "keep the load rule (layer-less conditions always visible)"
- closed (wont-fix): Won't fix. User decision 2026-11-16, option (b): "keep the load rule (layer-less conditions always visible)". Item reason: Windows itself is inconsistent for layer-less conditions (session-only hide versus visible again on reload) and real OST conditions normally have a layer, so the load rule stays: layer-less conditions are always visible.
- outcome (wont-fix): Closed by user decision: Won't fix. User decision 2026-11-16, option (b): "keep the load rule (layer-less conditions always visible)". Item reason: Windows itself is inconsistent for layer-less conditions (session-only hide versus visible again on reload) and real OST conditions normally have a layer, so the load rule stays: layer-less conditions are always visible. Files: `Core/Data/ProjectData.hpp:44-52; ViewController+Layers.mm:102-119`.
- possible Windows defect: ost_visualizer/domain/services/project_data_service.py:664-678; ost_visualizer/domain/services/project_data_service.py:645-662; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/bid_data_reader.py:927; ost_visualizer/presentation/dialogs/edit_condition_dialog.py:1150-1153; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/condition_operations.py:648-649; tests/domain/services/test_project_data_service.py:84-89; tests/domain/services/test_project_data_service.py:743-757: A layer-less condition is always visible on load, never touched by a single-layer hide, but hidden (and not placeable) in memory after Hide All until Show All or a reload. No Windows test pins Hide All for layer-less conditions. Scenario: c0 has no layer, c1 is on l1. Hide All: c0.layer_visible False and c1 False. Hide l1 only: c0 stays True. Reloading the project: c0 visible again. In-session c0 is greyed out, unplaceable and its meshes drop out until Show All. Severity: inconsistent, confidence: medium. Suggested Windows fix: Make update_all_layer_visibility skip conditions without a layer (as update_layer_visibility does), or make the load rule hide them too; then keep the snapshot rule consistent.

#### 172. QTY-19: Takeoffs with fewer than 2 position values are dropped at load, so count/attachment quantities can differ
- owner: database-persistence | impact: behavior | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- aliases: POS-M02
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/bid_data_reader.py:1116-1200 (position at 1124/1167); parse_position at domain/utils/position.py:5-18`: Every takeoff row is kept (empty or unparsable Position gives []). Count/attachment calc 23 still counts 1 for such a takeoff; linear/area give 0. A takeoff pointing at a missing condition aborts the load with an integrity error instead of being dropped.
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Load.cpp:264-267`: push_back only when the condition exists and position.size()>=2. parse_position returns {} on any unparsable token, so such takeoffs vanish and do not count. Missing-condition takeoffs are skipped silently.
- Windows tests: tests/domain/services/test_condition_quantity_service.py::ChildKindAndRoundingBoundaryTests::test_a_takeoff_without_geometry_totals_zero
- note (Phase 1): Likely a deliberate defensive filter; only the product owner can say whether a position-less count should show. Overlaps the persistence area.
- decision 2026-11-16: option (a) Keep them with an empty position (counts match Windows; PlanView and mesh code must tolerate empty positions). User: "keep takeoffs with empty positions (counts must match Windows). PlanView and mesh code must tolerate empty positions"
- fix scope (set by the decision, owner database-persistence): Keep takeoffs with fewer than 2 position values (empty position) at load so counts match Windows. PlanView, hit-testing and all mesh code must tolerate empty positions.
- outcome (fixed): load_project keeps EVERY takeoff row of the bid: an empty / NULL / unparsable / short Position is position [] (or the parsed values), no more filter on position.size() >= 2 or on the condition existing (a missing condition now fails the open, DBP-31). Windows values: three Count takeoffs with Position NULL, '' and '5;6' load as 3 takeoffs ([], [], [5,6]) and CALC_COUNT is 3;... Files: `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Load.cpp`.

#### 174. GEO-14: Area tessellation: earcut is fed a mirrored frame, so the triangle set and diagonals differ from Windows
- owner: meshing | impact: behavior | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/core/geometry/area.py:86-112`: The 2D ring handed to earcut is the 3D (X,Y) = (-x, +y) footprint of the bottom vertices (holes appended with hole start indices). Bottom triangles use the earcut order as-is; the top ring is reversed. Verified faces for the square [0,0,4,0,4,4,0,4] thickness 1: bottom (1,0,3),(3,2,1); top (7,4,5),(5,6,7).
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Meshing/gen_area.cpp:118-130`: polygon is built from vertices in the iOS (x,z) = (-x,-y) frame, which is the Windows frame mirrored in Y. Earcut ear selection and diagonals depend on orientation, so the same polygon gets different triangles: bottom (2,3,0),(0,1,2) (the other diagonal) for the square; holes and L-shapes also differ. ...
- Windows tests: tests/integration/native/test_geometry_boundaries.py::test_valid_earcut_and_curve_inputs_are_accepted
- note (Phase 1): Vertex arrays are already identical under the (X,Y,Z)->(X,Z,-Y) mapping, so only the earcut input frame and the winding (GEO-15) differ. Geometry (area, volume) is the same; this only affects face lists and ordering in exports and boolean tie-breaks. Overlaps with the meshing mapper.
- fix scope (set by the decision, owner meshing): Decisions 2026-11-16 (GEO-15 and EXP-17, option a): this item stays the only triangulation fix. Fix the triangulation sets only; add equivalence tests (same vertex set, same triangle sets, same bounding box, positive signed volume). No vertex-order or winding change: iOS meshes stay outward-facing.
- outcome (fixed): Implemented independent of GEO-15: gen_area.cpp now feeds earcut the Windows 3D footprint (x, -z) (outer ring and holes), so the triangle set (ear selection and diagonals) equals Windows; the cap triangles are emitted reversed relative to earcut's output (bottom (c,b,a), top (a,b,c)) which keeps the current outward-facing winding exactly (signed volume stays +16/+15/..., she... Files: `Core/Meshing/gen_area.cpp`.

- note (Phase 1): Platform-layer input adaptation needed to keep the Windows grammar usable; not verified on a device (no Xcode here).

#### 177. BRW-M01: Folder (project) display order is locale-aware/case-insensitive on Windows but SQLite BINARY / std::string < on iOS
- owner: project-browser | impact: behavior | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED (verifier-added) | status: fixed
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/presentation/components/project_tree_view.py:453-454 (sortByColumn(0, Ascending)) and :641-655 (_create_project_item builds a plain QTreeWidgetItem); ...`: Project nodes are plain QTreeWidgetItem, so the tree orders them with Qt's default item compare, which is locale-aware and case-insensitive at primary level (lowercase before uppercase on ties). Measured with PySide6 6.10.1 / Qt 6.10.1, locale en_US, offscreen, a plain QTreeWidget sorted ascending on column 0. ...
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Folders.cpp:29-31 (ORDER BY Name, SQLite BINARY, no UID tie-break); ...`: Binary byte order, so every uppercase name sorts before every lowercase name ('Zebra' before 'apple'). The mapper judged the folder ordering as 'matches (same as Qt)'. That was wrong for mixed-case, accented and punctuation names. The Windows tests only pin Alpha/Beta/Alpha, which hides the difference.
- Windows tests: tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_hierarchy_reader.py::test_full_hierarchy_maps_every_bid_field_and_orders_bids_and_projects (reader order only; no Windows test pins the Qt collation)
- note (Phase 1): Evidence is an empirical Qt run (the scripts were scratch files outside both repos and are not kept). The exact order is locale-dependent, so the user must decide how close to match. It also affects the page-selector folder order (BRW-17).
- decision 2026-11-16: option (a) Case-insensitive ASCII compare with tie-breaks (fold, then lowercase first, then UID). User: "case-insensitive ASCII compare, then lowercase first, then UID"
- fix scope (set by the decision, owner project-browser): Folder display order: case-insensitive ASCII compare, then lowercase first, then UID.
- outcome (fixed): Decided (a): folder display order = case-insensitive ASCII compare (only A-Z fold, other bytes unsigned), then lowercase first at the first differing byte, then UID ascending numerically. Implemented as ost_domain::compare_folder_names / compare_folder_uids / folder_precedes (header-only, reusable) and applied in load_hierarchy (SQL ORDER BY Name, UID + stable sort). Item pl... Files: `Core/Domain/DatabaseHierarchy.hpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Folders.cpp`.

#### 178. CAM-04: Pinch zoom clamp is a fixed 1..500 that the fit distance can exceed; Windows clamps relative to the scene
- owner: settings-undo-camera | impact: behavior | disposition: fix | testable: needs-extraction-from-objc | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: fixed
- Windows: `cpp_extensions/src/renderer/renderer.cpp:291-315 (Camera::zoom, configure_clip_planes)`: new_distance = clamp(distance - delta*distance*0.001, 2*near, 0.5*far), where near = max(0.1, 0.001*size) and far = max(10000, 20*size), so the limits scale with the scene. Native results from distance 100: zoom(+120) gives 88.0; zoom(-120) gives 112.0; zoom(+1e6) gives 0.2; zoom(-1e9) gives 5000.0.
- iOS (as mapped): `Controllers/ViewController/Components/CameraController.mm:108-121; Rendering/Renderer.mm:474-477`: cameraDistance is clamped to [1.0, 500.0] on every pinch change, while fitCameraToMeshes sets distance = 2.5*maxExtent with no upper bound. For maxExtent > 200 the first pinch event, even with zero delta, snaps the camera inward to 500.
- Windows tests: tests/integration/native/test_renderer_boundaries.py
- note (Phase 1): iOS near and far planes are computed per frame, so the clamp must be redefined together with them; the unit scale of the iOS scene was not verified here. Pinch rate (delta*dist*0.5) is platform-adapted.
- decision 2026-11-16: option (a) Scene-relative clamp using the Windows limits expressed in iOS scene units. User: "scene-relative zoom clamp with the Windows limits in iOS scene units"
- fix scope (set by the decision, owner settings-undo-camera): Scene-relative pinch-zoom clamp using the Windows limits expressed in iOS scene units (the fit distance can no longer exceed the clamp).
- outcome (fixed): Both wiring entries done: Renderer gets the read-only property sceneMaxExtent (largest side of the union of the mesh bounds, 0 without meshes; independent of _sceneRadius) and CameraController _handlePinch uses camera_clip_planes_for_extent(sceneMaxExtent) (default_camera_clip_planes() without meshes) and zoom_camera_distance(distance, delta, kPinchZoomRate, planes); the fix... Files: `Core/Domain/CameraLimits.hpp, Rendering/Renderer.h, Rendering/Renderer.mm, Controllers/ViewController/Components/CameraController.mm`.

#### 180. COND-STYLE-02: Style-change default spacing and shape retention differ
- owner: conditions | impact: behavior | disposition: fix | testable: needs-extraction-from-objc | verdict: AMBIGUOUS | status: fixed-ui-unverified
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/presentation/dialogs/edit_condition_dialog.py:90-143,1274-1276,1442-1448,1598-1602`: On a style change the DTO sets spacing = TYPE_DEFAULTS spacing (Linear 4.0, Area 6.0, Count 4.0, Attachment 4.0) and it stays unless the spacing field was edited to a value different from the stored spacing (then the field wins). Shape is reset to Square only when the change is not within {Count, Attachment}; Count <-> Attachment keeps the shape. ...
- iOS (as mapped): `EditConditionViewController.mm:350,393-420`: spacing = 4.0 for every type (Area should be 6.0); shape forced to Square for Count and Attachment even when switching between them.
- Windows tests: tests/presentation/dialogs/test_edit_condition_dialog.py::test_shared_fields_carry_over_and_type_specific_fields_get_defaults
- note (Phase 1): The 'unchanged spacing is replaced by the type default' rule is a Windows quirk (UI still shows the old text); it is deterministic and tested only indirectly, so flag it if the product intent is unclear. Verifier: Split finding. (a) Shape: CONFIRMED and clear. ...
- decision 2026-11-16: option (a) Persisted rule: type default (Area 6.0, others 4.0) unless the user edited the field. User: "on a style change, persist the type default spacing unless the user edited the field"
- fix scope (set by the decision, owner conditions): On a style change persist the type default spacing (Area 6.0, others 4.0) unless the user edited the spacing field.
- outcome (fixed-ui-unverified): The spacing rule of decision (a) of 2026-11-16 is unchanged: condition_to_save writes the TYPE_DEFAULTS spacing (Area 6.0 in / 150 mm, others 4.0 in / 100 mm), thickness and backout when the style differs from the stored one unless the field was edited; the shape is reset only when entering Count / Attachment from another style. DECIDED 2026-11-19 (code change, deliberate deviation from the literal Windows behavior): on a style change in a METRIC bid the metric type defaults are persisted, style_dimension_defaults(type, metric) (spacing 100 mm / Area 150 mm; thickness 100 / 100 / 100 / 0 mm), not the imperial table that Windows writes (edit_condition_dialog.py:1445-1448: possible Windows defect). condition_to_save(stored, form, metric) and type_change_defaults(type, metric) take the unit explicitly (no default argument); EditConditionViewController.mm passes the dialog _metric (one line); the imperial behavior is unchanged and equals Windows. Tests: CondStyle02_Spacing.* and CondM08_UnsupportedType.ASaveRewritesTheTypeWithTheMetricDefaultsInAMetricBid (failing first: 4 of 163 Conditions tests red with the old behavior); the test that pinned the literal Windows behavior was renamed and updated. Review: production code correct, one test gap closed by the reviewer (AMetricBidWritesTheMetricThicknessWhenTheFieldEqualsTheStoredOne). Unverified: the Edit Condition screen in a metric bid was not seen on a device. Files: `Core/Domain/ConditionStyleChange.cpp, Core/Domain/ConditionStyleChange.hpp, Controllers/EditConditionViewController/EditConditionViewController.mm, Config/Tests/Conditions/test_style_change.cpp`.
- decision (2026-11-19): code change, deliberate deviation from the literal Windows behavior: On a style change in a metric bid, the metric type defaults are persisted: style_dimension_defaults(type, metric) (spacing 100 mm / Area 150 mm; thickness 100 / 100 / 100 / 0 mm for Linear / Area / Count / Attachment), not the imperial table Windows writes (edit_condition_dialog.py:1445-1448). Deliberate deviation from the literal Windows behavior; the imperial behavior is unchanged and equals Windows. The spacing rule of the 2026-11-16 decision (a) is unchanged. So even with no negative in the export, the triangle and rhombus count shapes come out inside-out in the OBJ while every other shape is outward. The unused mesh_type argument of _transform_faces suggests per-type handling was intended. Vertex order and triangulation differences are not defects. The Windows test docstring itself records the table disagreement and that no sign contract exists. Scenario: A bid with a triangle-shaped count condition next to walls: in the exported OBJ the triangle prisms are inside-out (negative volume) and are culled or shaded wrongly by viewers that use back-face culling, while all other shapes look right. Suggested Windows fix: Make the generator tables consistent (reverse get_triangle_mesh_faces and get_rhombus_mesh_faces so they wind like the box and radial tables) or make _transform_faces use its mesh_type, and add a signed-volume test per shape.

The lighting normals are derived from the face winding (only +z is forced) and the viewer lights with the raw normal and no flip. Because the boolean wrapper flips untouched positives only when the export holds a negative of another type, the side-face normals of the same wall change sign depending on whether such a negative exists, so its lighting changes. Using inward-winding meshes with a raw-normal shader that matches the native renderer is a design choice; the data-dependent sign is the defect. Scenario: Export one wall to HTML: its vertical faces are lit from the inward-normal side. Add a negative area of another condition type anywhere in the selection and export again: the same wall's vertical faces are lit from the opposite side. Severity: inconsistent, confidence: medium. Suggested Windows fix: Same as EXP-13: flip only meshes that Manifold produced (flag boolean_applied) so winding, and therefore the shading normal, does not depend on whether any negative exists.

A legacy ANSI name containing a byte like 0x96 (en dash in cp1252) is invalid UTF-8, so [NSString stringWithUTF8String:] on the destination dir/file path returns nil. ...
- Windows tests: tests/integration/native/test_osp_cab.py::test_osp_imports_legacy_ansi_members_from_unicode_archive_path; tests/integration/native/test_osp_cab.py::test_native_cab_round_trip_preserves_utf8_paths_and_member_names
- note (Phase 1): CP_ACP is machine-dependent on Windows (the tests assume cp1252). The decision is which fixed code page iOS should assume for non-UTF-flagged names; cp1252 matches the Windows fixtures. ...
- decision 2026-11-16: option (a) cp1252 (matches the Windows fixtures). User: "cp1252 for names without the UTF-8 flag"
- fix scope (set by the decision, owner import-ost-osp): CAB member names without the UTF-8 flag are decoded as cp1252.
- outcome (fixed): list_cab / extract_cab / extract_cab_member / extract_cab_to_directory decode member names like cab_compressor.cpp decode_cab_member: attribs & 0x80 (_A_NAME_IS_UTF) -> strict UTF-8 (MB_ERR_INVALID_CHARS rules: no overlong forms, surrogates, > U+10FFFF, truncated or 5/6 byte forms; noncharacters accepted), otherwise cp1252 (the 5 undefined bytes 0x81 0x8D 0x8F 0x90 0x9D map ... Files: `Core/Import/CabMemberName.hpp, Core/Import/CabMemberName.cpp, Core/Import/CabExtractor.hpp, Core/Import/CabExtractor.cpp`.

- Windows: `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/importers/ost_importer.py:84-99; import_operations.py:33-82`: There is no duplicate check. Every import allocates new UIDs and a new BidNo, so importing the same file twice yields two bids.
- iOS (as mapped): `Services/ImportService.mm:68-83,289-304; Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Import.cpp:248-262`: If Bids.UID equals the file's Bid UID the import fails with 'This project is already imported'. This also blocks two different projects that happen to carry the same Bid UID, and it only guards the Bid row, not page/condition/takeoff UIDs (see IMP-06). For OSP it runs after the PDFs have already been written.
- Windows tests: tests/infrastructure/mdb/importers/test_ost_importer.py::test_import_ost_reports_exact_booleans_for_writer_results
- note (Phase 1): The guard is an iOS addition. Removing it is the Windows behavior, but it changes user-visible messaging, so I left the call to the user.
- decision 2026-11-16: option (a) Remove it (Windows behaviour; second import creates an independent copy with a new BidNo). User: "remove the "already imported" guard; each import is an independent copy"
- fix scope (set by the decision, owner import-ost-osp): Remove the "already imported" guard: each import is an independent copy (new BidNo).
- outcome (fixed): Both 'This project is already imported' guards are deleted and ost_store::is_bid_imported is removed from ProjectStore.hpp / ProjectStore+Import.cpp (nothing else called it). Files: `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore.hpp, Services/ImportService.mm, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore.cpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+Import.cpp`.

#### 192. IMP-20: DATETIME and binary column values stored as raw text
- owner: import-ost-osp | impact: behavior | disposition: wont-fix (Phase 1: fix; was needs-decision until 2026-11-19) | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED | status: wont-fix
- Windows: `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/import_operations.py:594-615`: DATETIME columns (14, e.g. Bids.CreateDateTime, ModDateTime, BidDate, CopyTimeStamp): a value with exactly 6 whitespace-separated integers 'Y M D h m s' becomes a datetime; anything else (blank, ISO, 3 parts, month 13, non-numeric) becomes NULL. Memo/IMAGE columns (30, e.g. ...
- iOS (as mapped): `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore_Internal.hpp:76-88`: Everything is stored as TEXT, so '2026 7 19 0 39 2' stays that string and invalid dates are kept instead of NULL. Binary columns hold UTF-8 text. Load reads bid_date/copy_timestamp as strings, and Settings.Created is written by create_schema as 'YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS', so the DB mixes two date formats.
- Windows tests: tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_import_operations.py::test_access_import_value_conversion_follows_access_column_types; tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_import_operations.py::test_datetime_text_needs_exactly_six_parts_otherwise_it_reads_as_null; tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_import_operations.py::test_only_annotation_text_tables_use_the_latin1_name_encoding
- note (Phase 1): The iOS schema has no datetime type, so which text format to store (raw 6-part string vs an ISO string like Settings.Created) is a product call that also affects any future OST export.
- decision 2026-11-16: option (a) Keep the raw 6-part string, but NULL for invalid values. User: "keep the raw 6-part string, NULL when invalid"
- fix scope (set by the decision, owner import-ost-osp): Keep the raw 6-part DATETIME string; store NULL when invalid.
- outcome (wont-fix): The decided DATETIME rule itself (raw 6-part string, NULL when invalid) and the memo text rule are fixed and tested (Imp20_* in test_import_column_types.cpp). DECIDED 2026-11-19 (keep iOS, no code change, all three sub-rules): (1) a DATETIME integer outside the C int range stores NULL and the import goes on (Windows import_operations.py:594-610 fails the whole import with OverflowError; possible Windows defect, low confidence); (2) BidTexts.Name and BidCallOuts.Name are stored as UTF-8 text (Windows import_operations.py:613-615 + serialization.py:7-8,70-73 store latin-1 bytes; not a defect on the import side, but the same latin-1 rule also rejects non-latin-1 annotation text inside Windows, annotation_operations.py:255-257: possible Windows defect); (3) memo and IMAGE text columns stay TEXT (Windows import_operations.py:616 stores a UTF-8 BLOB; not a defect). The numeric analogue IMP-05 (an integer above 64 bits fails like Windows) is unchanged. Files: `Core/Persistence/OstSchema.cpp, Core/Persistence/OstSchema.hpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/ProjectStore_ImportInternal.hpp, Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+ImportWriter.cpp, Core/Import/TextRules.hpp, Core/Import/TextRules.cpp`.
- decision (2026-11-19): keep iOS: (1) A DATETIME integer outside the C int range (for example "99999999999 1 1 0 0 0") stores NULL and the import goes on (Windows: OverflowError, the whole import fails and rolls back). No code change; the decided date rule itself (raw 6-part string, NULL when invalid) is fixed and tested. (2) BidTexts.Name and BidCallOuts.Name are stored as UTF-8 text (Windows: latin-1 bytes, raising for characters outside latin-1). No code change. (3) Memo and IMAGE (longbinary) text columns stay TEXT (Windows: UTF-8 BLOB). No code change. Pinned by: ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_AnIntegerBeyondTheCIntRangeReadsAsNullInsteadOfFailingTheImport; ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_AnOverflowingDateReadsAsNullAndTheImportGoesOn; ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_AnythingElseReadsAsNull; ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_AValidSixPartDateKeepsItsRawText; ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_TheConversionMatchesTheWindowsFunctionOnEveryOracleValue; ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_TheColumnTablesAreTheWindowsSchemaColumns; ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_MemoTextAndDatesOfTheBidTablesAreConverted; ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_EmptyMemoAndBinaryValuesAreNullAndTextIsKept; ImportColumnTypes.Imp20_TheTableInfoReportsTheWindowsTypeOfEveryDatetimeAndImageColumn. Test gap: Indirect only: BidTexts.Name and BidCallOuts.Name are IMAGE columns of the import tables (Imp20_TheColumnTablesAreTheWindowsSchemaColumns) and IMAGE text is kept as UTF-8 text (Imp20_MemoTextAndDatesOfTheBidTablesAreConverted checks other IMAGE columns); no test imports a BidTexts / BidCallOuts name with a character outside latin-1.
- possible Windows defect ((1) DATETIME integer beyond the C long range): ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/import_operations.py:594-610; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/import_operations.py:80-82; tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_import_operations.py:3044-3045; tests/infrastructure/mdb/components/test_import_operations.py:3079-3084: Bad date texts are handled in two ways: out-of-range values such as year 10000 or month 13 raise ValueError and read as NULL, while an integer beyond the C long range raises OverflowError, which is not caught, so one corrupt date aborts the entire import. It fails closed (rolled back, returns False), only a hand-made or corrupt file reaches it, and the Windows test itself calls it a characterisation with an open owner question (while the class docstring says overflow is meant to propagate), so intent is unclear. The threshold is also platform dependent (C long is 32-bit on Windows, 64-bit elsewhere). Scenario: An .ost whose DATETIME field holds '2147483648 1 1 0 0 0': the whole bid import is rolled back with a generic failure, while '10000 1 1 0 0 0' in the same place imports with a NULL date. Severity: inconsistent, confidence: low. Suggested Windows fix: Change line 608 to `except (ValueError, TypeError, OverflowError)` and flip the characterisation test (test_import_operations.py:3079) to expect None, if NULL like the other bad dates is wanted; otherwise document the fail-closed rule in the class docstring.
- possible Windows defect ((2b) latin-1 annotation names inside Windows): ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/annotation_operations.py:255-257; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/annotation_operations.py:465-467; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/annotation_operations.py:490-492; ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/annotation_operations.py:389-394; ost_visualizer/presentation/handlers/plan_view_action_handler.py:3473-3489: Beyond the import: the Windows text and callout annotation write path uses the same latin-1 encoder and no latin-1 guard exists in the UI (grep for latin-1 / cp1252 in ost_visualizer finds only serialization.py and parsers/utils/parser.py). Common pasted characters (euro sign, curly quotes, em dash, CJK) therefore fail at save time, and the warning that appears blames Page and Layer settings, not the text. Nothing is corrupted (fail-closed) and the refusal itself is test-pinned, so this is a usability inconsistency, not data loss. The full UI placement path was not traced end to end. Scenario: A Windows user pastes a text with a euro sign or curly quotes into a Text annotation: the annotation is not saved and the message points at Page and Layer settings. Severity: inconsistent, confidence: low. Suggested Windows fix: Validate the text against latin-1 in the annotation editor with a specific message, or, if the legacy OST reader really is Windows-1252, encode with cp1252 (covers euro and curly quotes) and keep the error only for the rest.

#### 193. IMP-M03: CAB extraction re-inflates and holds the entire folder in memory for every member
- owner: import-ost-osp | impact: behavior | disposition: fix | testable: core-cpp | verdict: CONFIRMED (verifier-added) | status: fixed

For an OSP with N PDFs in one folder the whole folder (all drawings) is decompressed N+1 times and held in RAM each time. Code reading only (not run); ...
- Windows tests: none found
- note (Phase 1): Not a Windows-parity rule; flagged because large OSPs (hundreds of MB of drawings) can exhaust iOS memory. Needs a decision on whether to stream per member or per folder.
- decision 2026-11-16: option (a) Single-pass extractor that streams each member to disk. User: "single-pass CAB extractor that streams to disk"
- fix scope (set by the decision, owner import-ost-osp): Single-pass CAB extractor that streams each member to disk (no whole-folder re-inflate held in memory for every member).
- outcome (fixed): One extract_cab_to_directory call into NSTemporaryDirectory()/ostv_osp_<random> (Core creates and always removes it), then the OST is read and the referenced images are copied from it; the std::transform(::tolower) calls on UTF-8 names are gone. The data is held with objc_precise_lifetime so the mapped bytes cannot be released early. Files: `Core/Import/CabExtractor.hpp, Core/Import/CabExtractor.cpp, Services/ImportService.mm`.
